\mathsf{F}
gives

[(\Delta\otimes\Delta){\overline{\mathsf{F}}}]=[(\mathrm{id}\otimes\Delta^{2}){\overline{\mathsf{F}}}][(1\otimes(\mathrm{id}\otimes\Delta){\overline{\mathsf{F}}}](\mathsf{F}\otimes 1\otimes 1)\;.

Then, identity (
4.18
) is equivalent to

\displaystyle[(1\otimes(\mathrm{id}\otimes\Delta)\mathsf{F}][(\mathrm{id}\otimes\Delta^{2})\mathsf{F}](1\otimes\mathsf{R}_{21}\otimes 1)(\mathrm{id}\otimes\tau\otimes\mathrm{id})\big[[(\mathrm{id}\otimes\Delta^{2}){\overline{\mathsf{F}}}][1\otimes(\mathrm{id}\otimes\Delta){\overline{\mathsf{F}}}]\big]

\displaystyle=(1\otimes 1\otimes{\overline{\mathsf{F}}})(1\otimes\mathsf{F}\,\mathsf{R}_{21}\,{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{21}\otimes 1)[(\mathrm{id}\otimes\tau\otimes\mathrm{id})(1\otimes 1\otimes\mathsf{F})]\;.

We multiply both sides by
(1\otimes 1\otimes\mathsf{F})
and use again the twist condition (
4.1
)
in the left hand side to simplify

(1\otimes\mathsf{F}\otimes 1)
and obtain

\displaystyle[(1\otimes(\Delta\otimes\mathrm{id})\mathsf{F}][(\mathrm{id}\otimes\Delta^{2})\mathsf{F}](1\otimes\mathsf{R}_{21}\otimes 1)(\mathrm{id}\otimes\tau\otimes\mathrm{id})\big[[(\mathrm{id}\otimes\Delta^{2}){\overline{\mathsf{F}}}][1\otimes(\mathrm{id}\otimes\Delta){\overline{\mathsf{F}}}]\big]

\displaystyle=(1\otimes\mathsf{R}_{21}\,{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{21}\otimes 1)[(\mathrm{id}\otimes\tau\otimes\mathrm{id})(1\otimes 1\otimes\mathsf{F})]\;.

The quasitriangular condition
\Delta^{cop}=\mathsf{R}\Delta{\overline{\mathsf{R}}}
gives

(\mathsf{R}_{21}\otimes 1)(\tau\otimes\mathrm{id})\Delta^{2}=(\mathsf{R}_{21}\Delta^{cop}\otimes\mathrm{id})\Delta=\Delta^{2}(\mathsf{R}_{21}\otimes 1)\;,

which we can use to simplify the left hand side to

[(1\otimes(\Delta\otimes\mathrm{id})\mathsf{F}](1\otimes\mathsf{R}_{21}\otimes 1)(\mathrm{id}\otimes\tau\otimes\mathrm{id})[1\otimes(\mathrm{id}\otimes\Delta){\overline{\mathsf{F}}}]

and further to

(1\otimes\mathsf{R}_{21}\otimes 1)[(1\otimes(\Delta^{cop}\otimes\mathrm{id})\mathsf{F}](\mathrm{id}\otimes\tau\otimes\mathrm{id})[1\otimes(\mathrm{id}\otimes\Delta){\overline{\mathsf{F}}}]

having used once again the quasitriangularity.
Summarizing, we are left to show that

[(1\otimes(\Delta^{cop}\otimes\mathrm{id})\mathsf{F}](\mathrm{id}\otimes\tau\otimes\mathrm{id})[1\otimes(\mathrm{id}\otimes\Delta){\overline{\mathsf{F}}}]\\
=(1\otimes{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{21}\otimes 1)[(\mathrm{id}\otimes\tau\otimes\mathrm{id})(1\otimes 1\otimes\mathsf{F})]

or, by (appropriately) simplifying the operator
(\mathrm{id}\otimes\tau\otimes\mathrm{id})
, that

[(1\otimes(\Delta\otimes\mathrm{id})\mathsf{F}][1\otimes(\mathrm{id}\otimes\Delta){\overline{\mathsf{F}}}]=(1\otimes{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}\otimes 1)(1\otimes 1\otimes\mathsf{F})\;.

The identity
[(\Delta\otimes\mathrm{id})\mathsf{F}][(\mathrm{id}\otimes\Delta){\overline{\mathsf{F}}}]=({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}\otimes 1)(1\otimes\mathsf{F})
follows from the twist condition (
4.1
), hence the identity (
4.18
) in the Proposition is proven.
∎

As mentioned, the twisted bialgebra
{K_{\mathsf{F}}}
is quasitriangular as well.

Proposition 4.8
.

If
(K,\mathsf{R})
is a quasitriangular bialgebra and
\mathsf{F}\in K\otimes K
is a twist for
K
, then the twisted bialgebra
{K_{\mathsf{F}}}
with
twisted coproduct
\Delta_{\mathsf{F}}
(see Proposition
4.4
) is quasitriangular with
\mathsf{R}
-matrix

(4.19)

{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}:=\mathsf{F}_{21}\,\mathsf{R}\,{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}=\mathsf{F}_{\alpha}\mathsf{R}^{\beta}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\gamma}\otimes\mathsf{F}^{\alpha}\mathsf{R}_{\beta}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\gamma}

and inverse
{{\overline{\mathsf{R}}}_{\mathsf{F}}}:=\mathsf{F}\,{\overline{\mathsf{R}}}\,{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{21}=\mathsf{F}^{\alpha}{\overline{\mathsf{R}}}^{\beta}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\gamma}\otimes\mathsf{F}_{\alpha}{\overline{\mathsf{R}}}_{\beta}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\gamma}
. If
(K,\mathsf{R})
is triangular, so is
({K_{\mathsf{F}}},{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}})
.
Also, if
K
is a quasitriangular Hopf algebra, then so is
{K_{\mathsf{F}}}
with twisted antipode
S_{\mathsf{F}}
.

The category
{}_{{K_{\mathsf{F}}}}{\mathcal{M}}
of left
{K_{\mathsf{F}}}
-modules is a braided monoidal category with braiding

(4.20)

\Psi^{{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}}_{V,W}:V\otimes W\longrightarrow W\otimes V\penalty\ ,\quad v\otimes w\longmapsto({\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright_{W}w)\otimes({\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright_{V}v)\penalty\ ,

for any pair of left
{K_{\mathsf{F}}}
-modules
(V,\vartriangleright_{V}),(W,\vartriangleright_{W})
.

Remark 4.9
.

The matrix
{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}
has been used in the Proposition
4.7
.
By inspection one sees that the identity (
4.17
) can be written as:

(4.21)

1\otimes\Psi^{{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}}_{{K_{\mathsf{F}}},{K_{\mathsf{F}}}}\otimes 1=(\mathsf{F}\otimes\mathsf{F})[(\Delta\otimes\Delta)\mathsf{F}][(1\otimes\Psi^{\mathsf{R}}_{K,K}\otimes 1)(\Delta\otimes\Delta){\overline{\mathsf{F}}}]({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}\otimes{\overline{\mathsf{F}}})\,.

Here
K
(respectively
{K_{\mathsf{F}}}
) is seen as a left
K
-module (respectively left
{K_{\mathsf{F}}}
-module).

For quasitriangular bialgebras we compare the monoidal categories of module algebras
({}_{K}{\mathcal{A}},\boxtimes)
and
({}_{{K_{\mathsf{F}}}}{\mathcal{A}},\boxtimes_{\mathsf{F}})
. Proposition
4.7
and Proposition
4.5
imply they are equivalent.

Proposition 4.10
.

Let
(K,\mathsf{R})
be a quasitriangular bialgebra and
\mathsf{F}\in K\otimes K
a twist.
There is an equivalence of monoidal categories between
({}_{K}{\mathcal{A}},\boxtimes)
and
({}_{{K_{\mathsf{F}}}}{\mathcal{A}},\boxtimes_{\mathsf{F}})

given by the functor
\Gamma:{}_{K}{\mathcal{A}}\to{}_{{K_{\mathsf{F}}}}{\mathcal{A}}
in (
4.9
) and the isomorphisms in
{}_{{K_{\mathsf{F}}}}{\mathcal{A}}

\displaystyle\varphi_{A,C}:A_{\mathsf{F}}\boxtimes_{\mathsf{F}}C_{\mathsf{F}}\longrightarrow(A\boxtimes C)_{\mathsf{F}}\penalty\ ,\quad a\boxtimes_{\mathsf{F}}c\longmapsto({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright_{A_{\mathsf{F}}}a)\boxtimes({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright_{C_{\mathsf{F}}}c)\penalty\ ,

with

A_{\mathsf{F}}\boxtimes_{\mathsf{F}}C_{\mathsf{F}}
the braided tensor product of the algebras
A_{\mathsf{F}}
and
C_{\mathsf{F}}
, and

(A\boxtimes C)_{\mathsf{F}}
the image via
\Gamma
of the braided tensor product of the algebras
A
and
C
.

Proof.

This is really a corollary of Proposition (
4.7
).
We need to show that the
isomorphisms
\varphi_{A,C}
in
{}_{{K_{\mathsf{F}}}}{\mathcal{M}}
, or equivalently their inverses

\varphi_{A,C}^{-1}:(A\boxtimes C)_{\mathsf{F}}\longrightarrow A_{\mathsf{F}}\boxtimes_{\mathsf{F}}C_{\mathsf{F}}\quad a\boxtimes c\longmapsto(\mathsf{F}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright_{A_{\mathsf{F}}}a)\boxtimes_{\mathsf{F}}(\mathsf{F}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright_{C_{\mathsf{F}}}c)\penalty\ ,

are algebra maps:

(4.22)

m_{A_{\mathsf{F}}\boxtimes_{\mathsf{F}}C_{\mathsf{F}}}\circ(\varphi_{A,C}^{-1}\otimes\varphi_{A,C}^{-1})=\varphi_{A,C}^{-1}\circ\,m_{(A\boxtimes C)_{\mathsf{F}}}\;.

To lighten notations, we omit the subscripts in the actions.
The product
m_{A_{\mathsf{F}}\boxtimes_{\mathsf{F}}C_{\mathsf{F}}}
in the l.h.s. is the product in the braided
tensor product algebra
A_{\mathsf{F}}\boxtimes_{\mathsf{F}}C_{\mathsf{F}}
, defined as in (
3.9
), but now with respect to the
\mathsf{R}
-matrix
{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}
:

\displaystyle m_{A_{\mathsf{F}}\boxtimes_{\mathsf{F}}C_{\mathsf{F}}}\left((a\boxtimes_{\mathsf{F}}c)\otimes(a^{\prime}\boxtimes_{\mathsf{F}}c^{\prime})\right)

\displaystyle=a{\,\raisebox{1.5pt}{\scalebox{0.55}[0.55]{\mbox{$\bullet_{\mbox{$\mathsf{F}\,$}}$}}}}({\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright a^{\prime})\boxtimes_{\mathsf{F}}({\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright c){\,\raisebox{1.5pt}{\scalebox{0.55}[0.55]{\mbox{$\bullet_{\mbox{$\mathsf{F}\,$}}$}}}}c^{\prime}\,,

\displaystyle=({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\beta}\vartriangleright a)({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\beta}{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright a^{\prime})\boxtimes_{\mathsf{F}}({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\gamma}{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright c)({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\gamma}\vartriangleright c^{\prime})\,.

The product
\,m_{(A\boxtimes C)_{\mathsf{F}}}
in the r.h.s. is the deformation (as in (
4
)) of the product

in the tensor
product algebra
A\boxtimes C
in (
3.9
) :

\displaystyle\,m_{(A\boxtimes C)_{\mathsf{F}}}\left((a\boxtimes c)\otimes(a^{\prime}\boxtimes c^{\prime})\right)

\displaystyle=\big({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright(a\boxtimes c)\big)\,{\raisebox{1.20554pt}{\rule{2.58334pt}{2.58334pt}}}\,\big({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright(a^{\prime}\boxtimes c^{\prime})\big)

\displaystyle=\big(({{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\vartriangleright a)\boxtimes({{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\vartriangleright c)\big)\,{\raisebox{1.20554pt}{\rule{2.58334pt}{2.58334pt}}}\,\big(({{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\vartriangleright a^{\prime})\boxtimes({{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\vartriangleright c^{\prime})\big)

\displaystyle=({{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\vartriangleright a)(\mathsf{R}_{\beta}{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\vartriangleright a^{\prime})\boxtimes(\mathsf{R}^{\beta}{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\vartriangleright c)({{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\vartriangleright c^{\prime})\,.

Then, the l.h.s. of (
4.22
) on the generic element
(a\boxtimes a^{\prime})\otimes(c\boxtimes c^{\prime})
gives

m_{A_{\mathsf{F}}\boxtimes_{\mathsf{F}}C_{\mathsf{F}}}(\varphi_{A,C}^{-1}(a\boxtimes c)\otimes\varphi_{A,C}^{-1}(a^{\prime}\boxtimes c^{\prime}))\\
=({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\beta}\mathsf{F}^{\gamma}\vartriangleright a)({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\beta}{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\mathsf{F}^{\delta}\vartriangleright a^{\prime})\boxtimes_{\mathsf{F}}({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\mu}{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\mathsf{F}_{\gamma}\vartriangleright c)({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\mu}\mathsf{F}_{\delta}\vartriangleright c^{\prime})\,.

Similarly, the r.h.s. of equation (
4.22
) gives

\varphi_{A,C}^{-1}\circ\,m_{(A\boxtimes C)_{\mathsf{F}}}((a\boxtimes a^{\prime})\otimes(c\boxtimes c^{\prime}))\\
=({\mathsf{F}^{\gamma}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\vartriangleright a)({\mathsf{F}^{\gamma}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\mathsf{R}_{\beta}{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\vartriangleright a^{\prime})\boxtimes_{\mathsf{F}}({\mathsf{F}_{\gamma}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\mathsf{R}^{\beta}{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\vartriangleright c)({\mathsf{F}_{\gamma}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\vartriangleright c^{\prime})\;.

Equation (
4.22
) then follows from (
4.18
) in Proposition
4.7
.
∎

4.3.
Braided bialgebras

Next consider a braided bialgebra
(L,m_{L},\eta_{L},\Delta_{L},\varepsilon_{L},\vartriangleright_{L})
,
associated with the quasitriangular bialgebra
(K,\mathsf{R})
.

On the one hand,
(L,m_{L},\eta_{L},\vartriangleright_{L})
is a
K
-module
algebra that we can deform using the functor in (
4.9
) into the

{K_{\mathsf{F}}}
-module algebra
({L_{\mathsf{F}}},m_{L_{\mathsf{F}}},\eta_{L_{\mathsf{F}}},\vartriangleright_{L_{\mathsf{F}}})
; this differs from
L
only for the product given by

\xi{\,\raisebox{1.5pt}{\scalebox{0.55}[0.55]{\mbox{$\bullet_{\mbox{$\mathsf{F}\,$}}$}}}}\xi^{\prime}=({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright\xi)\,({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright\xi^{\prime})
, for all
\xi,\xi^{\prime}\in L_{\mathsf{F}}
, as in (
4
).

On the other hand,
(L,\Delta_{L},\varepsilon_{L},\vartriangleright_{L})
is a

K
-module
coalgebra
that we deform using the functor in (
4.11
) so to obtain the

{K_{\mathsf{F}}}
-module coalgebra
({L_{\mathsf{F}}},\Delta_{L_{\mathsf{F}}},\varepsilon_{L_{\mathsf{F}}},\vartriangleright_{L_{\mathsf{F}}})
with twisted coproduct

\Delta_{L_{\mathsf{F}}}(\xi)={\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{[1]}}\otimes{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{[2]}}:=(\mathsf{F}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}})\otimes(\mathsf{F}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}})

for each
\xi\in L_{\mathsf{F}}
,
as in (
4.12
).

We have the following result (cf.
[
6
, Prop. 4.7]
for a dual result):

Proposition 4.11
.

The twist deformation of
(L,m_{L},\eta_{L},\vartriangleright_{L})

as a
K
-module algebra and of

(L,\Delta_{L},\varepsilon_{L},\vartriangleright_{L})
as a
K
-module coalgebra
is a braided bialgebra
associated with the twisted Hopf algebra
{K_{\mathsf{F}}}
. That is,
({L_{\mathsf{F}}},m_{L_{\mathsf{F}}},\eta_{L_{\mathsf{F}}},\Delta_{L_{\mathsf{F}}},\varepsilon_{L_{\mathsf{F}}},\vartriangleright_{L_{\mathsf{F}}})
is a bialgebra in the braided monoidal category
({}_{{K_{\mathsf{F}}}}{\mathcal{M}},\otimes_{\mathsf{F}},\Psi^{{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}})
of
{K_{\mathsf{F}}}
-modules.
If
L
is a braided Hopf algebra, then
L_{\mathsf{F}}
is a braided Hopf algebra, with
antipode
S_{L_{\mathsf{F}}}=\Gamma(S_{L})
(equal to
S_{L}
as a linear map).

Proof.

We have to show that
\Delta_{L_{\mathsf{F}}}:L_{\mathsf{F}}\to L_{\mathsf{F}}\boxtimes_{\mathsf{F}}L_{\mathsf{F}}
and
\varepsilon_{L_{\mathsf{F}}}:L_{\mathsf{F}}\to\mathds{k}

are algebra maps:

(4.23)

\varepsilon_{L_{\mathsf{F}}}\circ m_{L_{\mathsf{F}}}=m_{\mathds{k}}\circ(\varepsilon_{L_{\mathsf{F}}}\otimes\varepsilon_{L_{\mathsf{F}}})\,,\quad\Delta_{L_{\mathsf{F}}}\circ m_{L_{\mathsf{F}}}=m_{{L_{\mathsf{F}}}\boxtimes_{\mathsf{F}}{L_{\mathsf{F}}}}\circ(\Delta_{L_{\mathsf{F}}}\otimes_{\mathsf{F}}\Delta_{L_{\mathsf{F}}})\,,

for the product in
L_{\mathsf{F}}
,

m_{L_{\mathsf{F}}}(\xi\otimes\zeta)=({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright\xi)\,({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright\zeta)
, with
\xi,\zeta\in L_{\mathsf{F}}

and
the product
m_{{L_{\mathsf{F}}}\boxtimes_{\mathsf{F}}{L_{\mathsf{F}}}}=(m_{L_{\mathsf{F}}}\otimes_{\mathsf{F}}m_{L_{\mathsf{F}}})\circ(\mathrm{id}_{L_{\mathsf{F}}}\otimes_{\mathsf{F}}\Psi^{{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}}_{{L_{\mathsf{F}}},{L_{\mathsf{F}}}}\otimes_{\mathsf{F}}\mathrm{id}_{L_{\mathsf{F}}})
in the braided tensor product algebra
{L_{\mathsf{F}}}\boxtimes_{\mathsf{F}}{L_{\mathsf{F}}}
. This latter is given from (
3.9
) by

m_{L_{\mathsf{F}}\boxtimes_{\mathsf{F}}L_{\mathsf{F}}}\left((\xi\boxtimes_{\mathsf{F}}\zeta)\otimes(\xi^{\prime}\boxtimes_{\mathsf{F}}\zeta^{\prime})\right)=({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\beta}\vartriangleright\xi)({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\beta}{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright\xi^{\prime})\boxtimes_{\mathsf{F}}({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\gamma}{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright\zeta)({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\gamma}\vartriangleright\zeta^{\prime})\,.

The identity involving the counit follows from the algebra map property of
\varepsilon_{L}
, by using the module coalgebra axiom (
2.6
) and the fact that
\mathsf{F}
is unital.

For the second identity, with coproduct
\Delta_{L_{\mathsf{F}}}:\xi\mapsto{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{[1]}}\otimes{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{[2]}}:=(\mathsf{F}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}})\otimes(\mathsf{F}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}})
,
we compute the l.h.s. by using the module coalgebra axiom (
2.6
) and the property of the coproduct
\Delta_{L}
to be a braided algebra map: on the generic element
\xi\otimes\zeta
one has

\displaystyle l.h.s.

\displaystyle=\Delta_{L_{\mathsf{F}}}\circ m_{L_{\mathsf{F}}}(\xi\otimes\zeta)

\displaystyle=\Delta_{L_{\mathsf{F}}}\left(({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright\xi)\,({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright\zeta)\right)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{F}^{\gamma}\vartriangleright\big({({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright\xi)}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\mathsf{R}_{\beta}\vartriangleright{({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright\zeta)}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\big)\otimes\mathsf{F}_{\gamma}\vartriangleright\big(\mathsf{R}^{\beta}\vartriangleright{({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright\xi)}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}{({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright\zeta)}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{F}^{\gamma}\vartriangleright\big(({{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}})(\mathsf{R}_{\beta}{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\vartriangleright{\zeta}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}})\big)\otimes\mathsf{F}_{\gamma}\vartriangleright\big((\mathsf{R}^{\beta}{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}({{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\vartriangleright{\zeta}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}})\big)

\displaystyle=({\mathsf{F}^{\gamma}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}})({\mathsf{F}^{\gamma}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\mathsf{R}_{\beta}{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\vartriangleright{\zeta}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}})\otimes({\mathsf{F}_{\gamma}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\mathsf{R}^{\beta}{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}})\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}({\mathsf{F}_{\gamma}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}})\vartriangleright{\zeta}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\;.

Similarly, we compute the r.h.s.:

\displaystyle r.h.s.

\displaystyle=m_{{L_{\mathsf{F}}}\boxtimes_{\mathsf{F}}{L_{\mathsf{F}}}}\circ(\Delta_{L_{\mathsf{F}}}\otimes_{\mathsf{F}}\Delta_{L_{\mathsf{F}}})(\xi\otimes\zeta)

\displaystyle=({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\beta}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{[1]}})({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\beta}{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright{\zeta}_{\scriptscriptstyle{[1]}})\boxtimes_{\mathsf{F}}({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\gamma}{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{[2]}})({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\gamma}\vartriangleright{\zeta}_{\scriptscriptstyle{[2]}})

\displaystyle=({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\beta}\mathsf{F}^{\gamma}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}})({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\beta}{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\mathsf{F}^{\delta}\vartriangleright{\zeta}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}})\boxtimes_{\mathsf{F}}({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\mu}{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\mathsf{F}_{\gamma}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}})({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\mu}\mathsf{F}_{\delta}\vartriangleright{\zeta}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}})\;.

The two expressions coincide from (
4.18
) in Proposition
4.7
.

If
L
is a Hopf algebra with antipode
S_{L}
, which is a
K
-module map,
its image under
\Gamma
,
\xi\mapsto S_{L_{\mathsf{F}}}(\xi)=S_{L}(\xi)
, is a
K_{\mathsf{F}}
-module map. We have only to show that
S_{L_{\mathsf{F}}}
satisfies the antipode conditions (
3.11
) for
the twisted bialgebra
L_{\mathsf{F}}
. For any element
\xi\in L_{\mathsf{F}}
one has

\displaystyle m_{L_{\mathsf{F}}}\circ(\mathrm{id}_{L_{\mathsf{F}}}\otimes_{\mathsf{F}}S_{L_{\mathsf{F}}})\circ\Delta_{L_{\mathsf{F}}}(\xi)

\displaystyle={\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright(\mathsf{F}^{\beta}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}})\otimes{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright(S_{L}(\mathsf{F}_{\beta}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}))

\displaystyle=({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\mathsf{F}^{\beta})\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\otimes({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\mathsf{F}_{\beta})\vartriangleright S_{L}({\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}})=\varepsilon_{L_{\mathsf{F}}}(\xi)1_{L_{\mathsf{F}}}\;.

Analogously one shows that

m_{L_{\mathsf{F}}}\circ(S_{L_{\mathsf{F}}}\otimes_{\mathsf{F}}\mathrm{id}_{L_{\mathsf{F}}})\circ\Delta_{L_{\mathsf{F}}}=\eta_{L_{\mathsf{F}}}\circ\varepsilon_{L_{\mathsf{F}}}
.
∎

Example 4.12
.

Let
\underline{K}:=(K,m_{K},\eta_{K},\underline{\Delta},\varepsilon_{K},\underline{S},\vartriangleright_{\mathsf{ad}})
be the braided Hopf algebra associated with the quasitriangular Hopf algebra
(K,\mathsf{R})
, as in Example
3.5
.

If
\mathsf{F}
is a twist for
K
, the data

(4.24)

\underline{K}_{\mathsf{F}}:=(\underline{K},m_{K_{\mathsf{F}}},\eta_{K_{\mathsf{F}}},\underline{\Delta}_{\mathsf{F}},\varepsilon_{K_{\mathsf{F}}},\underline{S}_{\mathsf{F}},\vartriangleright_{\mathsf{ad}})

is a braided Hopf algebra associated with
{K_{\mathsf{F}}}
. From the construction above,
\underline{K}_{\mathsf{F}}
has the
same unit as
\underline{K}
while its product is the deformed one as in (
4
):

m_{K_{\mathsf{F}}}(h\otimes k):=h{\,\raisebox{1.5pt}{\scalebox{0.55}[0.55]{\mbox{$\bullet_{\mbox{$\mathsf{F}\,$}}$}}}}k=({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright h)\,({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright k)

Its coproduct is the deformed one as in (
4.12
):

\underline{\Delta}_{\mathsf{F}}(k):=\mathsf{F}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright_{\mathsf{ad}}({k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}S(\mathsf{R}_{\beta}))\otimes(\mathsf{F}_{\alpha}\mathsf{R}^{\beta})\vartriangleright_{\mathsf{ad}}{k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\,.

The left adjoint action of
{K_{\mathsf{F}}}
on
\underline{K}_{\mathsf{F}}
is the initial (unchanged) left adjoint action,

\vartriangleright_{\mathsf{ad}}:{K_{\mathsf{F}}}\otimes\underline{K}_{\mathsf{F}}\to\underline{K}_{\mathsf{F}}\;,\quad h\otimes k\mapsto h\vartriangleright_{\mathsf{ad}}k:={h}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}kS({h}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}).

Finally, the antipode
\underline{S}_{\mathsf{F}}
of
\underline{K}_{\mathsf{F}}
coincides with that of
\underline{K}
,

\underline{S}_{\mathsf{F}}:k\mapsto\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}S(\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright_{\mathsf{ad}}k)
, as a linear map.

\blacksquare

Example 4.13
.

Rather than first considering the braided Hopf algebra
\underline{K}
and then
twisting it to
\underline{K}_{\mathsf{F}}
, one could start with the quasitriangular
Hopf algebra
{K_{\mathsf{F}}}
and associate to it the braided Hopf algebra

\underline{{K_{\mathsf{F}}}}:=({K_{\mathsf{F}}},m_{K_{\mathsf{F}}},\eta_{K_{\mathsf{F}}},\underline{\Delta_{\mathsf{F}}},\varepsilon_{K_{\mathsf{F}}},\underline{S_{\mathsf{F}}},\vartriangleright_{\mathsf{ad}_{\mathsf{F}}})\penalty\ ,

again constructed as in Example
3.5
.
This consists of the algebra
({K_{\mathsf{F}}},m_{K_{\mathsf{F}}},\eta_{K_{\mathsf{F}}})
endowed with the same counit as
{K_{\mathsf{F}}}
and with the braided coproduct

(4.25)

\underline{\Delta_{\mathsf{F}}}(k):={k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{[1]}}S_{\mathsf{F}}({\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha})\otimes({\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright_{\mathsf{ad}_{\mathsf{F}}}{k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{[2]}})\,,

where
\Delta_{\mathsf{F}}(k)={k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{[1]}}\otimes{k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{[2]}}=\mathsf{F}^{\alpha}{k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\beta}\otimes\mathsf{F}_{\alpha}{k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\beta}
is the coproduct (
4.5
) of
{K_{\mathsf{F}}}
. Here the left adjoint action is

(4.26)

\vartriangleright_{\mathsf{ad}_{\mathsf{F}}}:{K_{\mathsf{F}}}\otimes\underline{{K_{\mathsf{F}}}}\to\underline{{K_{\mathsf{F}}}}\;,\quad h\otimes k\mapsto h\vartriangleright_{\mathsf{ad}_{\mathsf{F}}}k:={h}_{\scriptscriptstyle{[1]}}kS_{F}({h}_{\scriptscriptstyle{[2]}}),

with

S_{\mathsf{F}}
the antipode of
{K_{\mathsf{F}}}
:
S_{\mathsf{F}}(k)=\mathsf{u}_{\mathsf{F}}S(k)\bar{\mathsf{u}}_{\mathsf{F}}=\mathsf{F}^{\alpha}S(\mathsf{F}_{\alpha})S(k)S({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\beta}){\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\beta}
.
On elements,

h\otimes k\mapsto h\vartriangleright_{\mathsf{ad}_{\mathsf{F}}}k:=\mathsf{F}^{\alpha}{h}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\beta}\,k\,\mathsf{F}^{\gamma}S(\mathsf{F}_{\gamma})S(\mathsf{F}_{\alpha}{h}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\beta})S({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\delta}){\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\delta}\,.

With these structures
\underline{{K_{\mathsf{F}}}}
is a
{K_{\mathsf{F}}}
module algebra and module coalgebra, moreover

\underline{\Delta_{\mathsf{F}}}

is an algebra map (with respect to the braided tensor product). The antipode is given by

(4.27)

\underline{S_{\mathsf{F}}}:\underline{{K_{\mathsf{F}}}}\to\underline{{K_{\mathsf{F}}}}\;,\quad k\mapsto\underline{S_{\mathsf{F}}}(k):={\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}S_{\mathsf{F}}({\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright_{\mathsf{ad}_{\mathsf{F}}}k)

and is a left
{K_{\mathsf{F}}}
-module morphism.

\blacksquare

The following Proposition is dual to Theorem 4.9 in
[
6
]
; its
proof is in Appendix
A
.

Proposition 4.14
.

The two braided Hopf algebras
\underline{K}_{\mathsf{F}}
and
\underline{{K_{\mathsf{F}}}}
associated with
{K_{\mathsf{F}}}
are isomorphic via the map (cf. (
4.6
))

(4.28)

\mathcal{D}:\underline{K}_{\mathsf{F}}\to\underline{{K_{\mathsf{F}}}}\;,\quad k\mapsto\mathcal{D}(k):=({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright_{\mathsf{ad}}k){\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}=\mathsf{F}^{\alpha}kS(\mathsf{F}_{\alpha})\bar{\mathsf{u}}_{\mathsf{F}}

with inverse

(4.29)

\mathcal{D}^{-1}:\underline{{K_{\mathsf{F}}}}\to\underline{K}_{\mathsf{F}}\;,\quad k\mapsto\mathcal{D}^{-1}(k)=(\mathsf{F}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright_{\mathsf{ad}_{\mathsf{F}}}k)\mathsf{F}_{\alpha}={\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}kS_{\mathsf{F}}({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha})\mathsf{u}_{\mathsf{F}}\penalty\ .

4.4.
Dual pairings of braided bialgebras and their twisting

Definition 4.15
.

A dual pairing of two braided
bialgebras

(L,m_{L},\eta_{L},\Delta_{L},\varepsilon_{L},\vartriangleright_{L})
and

(N,m_{N},\eta_{N},\Delta_{N},\varepsilon_{N},\vartriangleright_{N})
associated with the quasitriangular bialgebra
(K,\mathsf{R})
is a bilinear map
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle:L\otimes N\to\mathds{k}
such that

\displaystyle\langle\xi,xy\rangle=\langle\Delta_{L}{\xi},x\otimes y\rangle:=\langle{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}},\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright_{N}x\rangle\langle\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright_{L}{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}},y\rangle

(4.30)

\displaystyle\langle\xi\eta,x\rangle=\langle\xi\otimes\eta,\Delta_{N}{x}\rangle:=\langle\xi,\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright_{N}{x}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\rangle\langle\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright_{L}\eta,{x}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\rangle

and

(4.31)

\langle\xi,1_{N}\rangle=\varepsilon_{L}(\xi)\;,\quad\langle 1_{L},x\rangle=\varepsilon_{N}(x)

for all
\xi,\penalty\ \eta\in L
and
x,\penalty\ y\in N
. Moreover
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle:L\otimes N\to\mathds{k}
is a morphism of
K
-modules:

(4.32)

\langle{k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\vartriangleright_{L}\xi,{k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\vartriangleright_{N}x\rangle=\varepsilon(k)\langle\xi,x\rangle

for each
k\in K
,
\xi\in L
and
x\in N
.
We say that
L
and
N
are dually paired if they admit a dual pairing.

Lemma 4.16
.

When
K
is a Hopf algebra, the condition for the pairing

\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
to be a morphism of
K
-modules can
be given equivalently as

(4.33)

\langle{k}\vartriangleright_{L}\xi,x\rangle=\langle\xi,S(k)\vartriangleright_{N}x\rangle\,,\quad\forall\penalty\ \xi\in L\;,x\in N\;,

or, with
S^{-1}
the inverse of the antipode of
K
,

(4.34)

\langle\xi,k\vartriangleright_{N}x\rangle=\langle S^{-1}(k)\vartriangleright_{L}\xi,x\rangle\,,\quad\forall\penalty\ \xi\in L\;,x\in N\;.

Proof.

If (
4.32
) holds, then for all
\xi\in L\;,x\in N
we have

\langle{k}\vartriangleright_{L}\xi,x\rangle=\langle{k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\vartriangleright_{L}\xi,\varepsilon({k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}})\vartriangleright_{N}x\rangle=\langle{k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\vartriangleright_{L}\xi,{k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\vartriangleright_{N}(S({k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(3)}})\vartriangleright_{N}x)\rangle=\langle\xi,S(k)\vartriangleright_{N}x\rangle.

Conversely, if (
4.33
) holds, then

\langle{k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\vartriangleright_{L}\xi,{k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\vartriangleright_{N}x\rangle=\langle\xi,S({k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}})\vartriangleright_{N}({k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\vartriangleright_{N}x)\rangle=\langle\xi,(S({k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}){k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}})\vartriangleright_{N}x\rangle=\varepsilon(k)\langle\xi,x\rangle.

Being the antipode of a quasitriangular Hopf algebra invertible (see (
3.7
)), identity (
4.33
) gives (
4.34
), when replacing
k
by
S^{-1}(k)
.
∎

Lemma 4.17
.

If
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle:L\otimes N\to\mathds{k}
is a dual pairing between two braided Hopf algebras
L
and
N
associated with the quasitriangular Hopf algebra
(K,\mathsf{R})
, then

(4.35)

\langle S_{L}(\xi),x\rangle=\langle\xi,S_{N}(x)\rangle\,,\quad\forall\penalty\ \xi\in L\;,x\in N\;.

Proof.

We omit the subscripts
L
and
N
. Using the pairing axioms (
4.15
), we compute

\displaystyle\langle S(\xi),x\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle S({\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}),\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright{x}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\rangle\varepsilon(\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}})\varepsilon({x}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}})

\displaystyle=\langle S({\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}),\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright{x}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\rangle\langle\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}},{x}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}S({x}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(3)}})\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle S({\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}),\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright{x}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\rangle\langle{(\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}})}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}},\mathsf{R}_{\beta}\vartriangleright{x}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\rangle\langle\mathsf{R}^{\beta}\vartriangleright{(\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}})}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}},S({x}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(3)}})\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle S({\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}),\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright{x}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\rangle\langle{\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}},\mathsf{R}_{\beta}\vartriangleright{x}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\rangle\langle(\mathsf{R}^{\beta}{\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}})\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(3)}},S({x}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(3)}})\rangle

where we used the module coalgebra axioms (
2.6
) in the last equality. Next, we use the quasitriangularity condition
(\Delta\otimes\mathrm{id})\mathsf{R}=\mathsf{R}_{13}\mathsf{R}_{23}
and get

\displaystyle\langle S(\xi),x\rangle=\langle S({\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}),\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\mathsf{R}_{\gamma}\vartriangleright{x}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\rangle\langle{\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}},\mathsf{R}_{\beta}\vartriangleright{x}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\rangle\langle(\mathsf{R}^{\beta}{\mathsf{R}^{\gamma}})\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(3)}},S({x}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(3)}})\rangle.

On the other hand, with analogous computations we have

\displaystyle\langle\xi,S(x)\rangle

\displaystyle=\varepsilon({\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}})\varepsilon(\mathsf{R}_{\gamma}\vartriangleright{x}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}})\langle\mathsf{R}^{\gamma}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}},S({x}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}})\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle S({\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}){\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}},\mathsf{R}_{\gamma}\vartriangleright{x}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\rangle\langle\mathsf{R}^{\gamma}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(3)}},S({x}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}})\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle S({\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}),\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright{(\mathsf{R}_{\gamma}\vartriangleright{x}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}})}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\rangle\langle\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}},{(\mathsf{R}_{\gamma}\vartriangleright{x}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}})}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\rangle\langle\mathsf{R}^{\gamma}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(3)}},S({x}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}})\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle S({\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}),\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}{\mathsf{R}_{\gamma}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\vartriangleright{x}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\rangle\langle\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}},{\mathsf{R}_{\gamma}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\vartriangleright{x}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\rangle\langle\mathsf{R}^{\gamma}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(3)}},S({x}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(3)}})\rangle.

Finally, the quasitriangular condition
(\mathrm{id}\otimes\Delta)\mathsf{R}=\mathsf{R}_{13}\mathsf{R}_{12}
gives

\displaystyle\langle\xi,S(x)\rangle=\langle S({\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}),\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}{\mathsf{R}_{\gamma}}\vartriangleright{x}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\rangle\langle\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}},{\mathsf{R}_{\beta}}\vartriangleright{x}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\rangle\langle\mathsf{R}^{\beta}\mathsf{R}^{\gamma}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(3)}},S({x}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(3)}})\rangle=\langle\xi,S(x)\rangle,

the stated identity. ∎

Proposition 4.18
.

Let
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle:L\otimes N\to\mathds{k}
be a dual pairing between two braided
bialgebras

(L,m_{L},\eta_{L},\Delta_{L},\varepsilon_{L},\vartriangleright_{L})
and

(N,m_{N},\eta_{N},\Delta_{N},\varepsilon_{N},\vartriangleright_{N})
associated with the quasitriangular bialgebra
(K,\mathsf{R})
.
Then
the braided bialgebras

({L_{\mathsf{F}}},m_{L_{\mathsf{F}}},\eta_{L_{\mathsf{F}}},\Delta_{L_{\mathsf{F}}},\varepsilon_{L_{\mathsf{F}}},\vartriangleright_{L_{\mathsf{F}}})
and

({N_{\mathsf{F}}},m_{N_{\mathsf{F}}},\eta_{N_{\mathsf{F}}},\Delta_{N_{\mathsf{F}}},\varepsilon_{N_{\mathsf{F}}},\vartriangleright_{N_{\mathsf{F}}})
associated with
the quasitriangular bialgebra
({K_{\mathsf{F}}},{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}})
(as given in Proposition
4.11
)
are dually paired via

(4.36)

\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\mathsf{F}}:L_{\mathsf{F}}\otimes N_{\mathsf{F}}\to\mathds{k}\;,\quad\xi\otimes_{\mathsf{F}}x\mapsto\langle\xi,x\rangle_{\mathsf{F}}:=\langle{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright_{L}\xi,{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright_{N}x\rangle

Proof.

Firstly we show that this map

\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\mathsf{F}}

is a
{K_{\mathsf{F}}}
-module map. With the coproduct for
{K_{\mathsf{F}}}
given in (
4.5
),
we have

\displaystyle\langle{k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{[1]}}\vartriangleright_{L}\xi,{k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{[2]}}\vartriangleright_{N}x\rangle_{\mathsf{F}}

\displaystyle=\langle\mathsf{F}^{\alpha}{k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\beta}\vartriangleright_{L}\xi,\mathsf{F}_{\alpha}{k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\beta}\vartriangleright_{N}x\rangle_{\mathsf{F}}=\langle{k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\beta}\vartriangleright_{L}\xi,{k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\beta}\vartriangleright_{N}x\rangle

\displaystyle=\varepsilon(k)\langle{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\beta}\vartriangleright_{L}\xi,{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\beta}\vartriangleright_{N}x\rangle=\varepsilon(k)\langle\xi,x\rangle_{\mathsf{F}}

where we have used that the pairing
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle:L\otimes N\to\mathds{k}
is a
K
-module map.
Next, being
F
unital, the compatibility properties (
4.31
)
with the counits are easily verified.
We show the first of properties (
4.15
), the other one can be established similarly.
To lighten the notation we omit the subscripts. We have

\displaystyle\langle\xi,x{\,\raisebox{1.5pt}{\scalebox{0.55}[0.55]{\mbox{$\bullet_{\mbox{$\mathsf{F}\,$}}$}}}}y\rangle_{\mathsf{F}}

\displaystyle=\langle{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright\xi,{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright\big(({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\beta}\vartriangleright x)({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\beta}\vartriangleright y)\big)\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright\xi,({{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\beta}\vartriangleright x)({{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\beta}\vartriangleright y)\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\beta}\vartriangleright\xi,({{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\beta}\vartriangleright x)({{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}}\vartriangleright y)\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\beta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}},\mathsf{R}_{\gamma}{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(3)}}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\beta}\vartriangleright x\rangle\langle\mathsf{R}^{\gamma}{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\beta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}},{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}}\vartriangleright y\rangle

where for the last two identities we have used the twist condition (
4.4
), followed by the module coalgebra condition (
2.6
).
This can be further simplified by using the quasitriangular condition (
3.1
) of the coproduct of
K
and then the
property (
4.32
) for the pairing to be a
K
-module map:

\langle{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\beta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}},{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\mathsf{R}_{\gamma}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\beta}\vartriangleright x\rangle\langle{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(3)}}\mathsf{R}^{\gamma}{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\beta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}},{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}}\vartriangleright y\rangle\\
=\langle{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\beta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}},\mathsf{R}_{\gamma}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\beta}\vartriangleright x\rangle\langle{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}}\mathsf{R}^{\gamma}{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\beta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}},{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}}\vartriangleright y\rangle\,.

By using again the twist condition and the coproduct in
L_{\mathsf{F}}
, we have

\displaystyle\langle{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\beta}}\mathsf{F}^{\tau}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}},\mathsf{R}_{\gamma}{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\beta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\mu}\vartriangleright x\rangle

\displaystyle\langle{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}}\mathsf{R}^{\gamma}{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\beta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\mu}\mathsf{F}_{\tau}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}},{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}}\vartriangleright y\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\beta}}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{[1]}},\mathsf{R}_{\gamma}{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\beta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\mu}\vartriangleright x\rangle\langle{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}}\mathsf{R}^{\gamma}{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\beta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\mu}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{[2]}},{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}}\vartriangleright y\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\beta}}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{[1]}},{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\beta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\mathsf{R}_{\gamma}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\mu}\vartriangleright x\rangle\langle{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}}{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\beta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\mathsf{R}^{\gamma}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\mu}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{[2]}},{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}}\vartriangleright y\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\beta}}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{[1]}},{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\beta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\mu}{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{\gamma}\vartriangleright x\rangle\langle{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}}{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\beta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\mu}{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\gamma}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{[2]}},{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}}\vartriangleright y\rangle

where in the last but one equality we have used once again the quasitriangular condition (
3.1
) in the Definition
3.1
,
of the coproduct and in the last one the definition of
{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}
. Then, the twist condition and the

K
-module map property (
4.32
) of the pairing yields

\displaystyle\langle{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\mu}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\beta}}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{[1]}},{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\mu}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\beta}}{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{\gamma}\vartriangleright x\rangle

\displaystyle\langle{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\mu}{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\gamma}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{[2]}},{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}}\vartriangleright y\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\beta}}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{[1]}},{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\beta}}{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{\gamma}\vartriangleright x\rangle\langle{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}}{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\gamma}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{[2]}},{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}}\vartriangleright y\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{[1]}},{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright x\rangle_{\mathsf{F}}\langle{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{[2]}},y\rangle_{\mathsf{F}}\;,

thus concluding the proof.
∎

5.
Braided Lie algebras and their twisting

Further insights in the use of braided Hopf algebras as algebras of symmetries come from the
study of associated braided Lie algebras: Lie algebras in the braided
monoidal category of
K
-modules, with
(K,\mathsf{R})

triangular.

5.1.
Braided Lie algebras

Definition 5.1
.

A
braided Lie algebra
associated with a triangular
Hopf algebra
(K,\mathsf{R})
, or simply a
K
-braided Lie algebra,
is a
K
-module
\mathfrak{g}
with a
bilinear map (a nonassociative non-unital multiplication)

[\penalty\ ,\penalty\ ]:\mathfrak{g}\otimes\mathfrak{g}\to\mathfrak{g}

that satisfies the conditions:

(i)

K
-equivariance:

k\vartriangleright[u,v]=[{k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\vartriangleright u,{k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\vartriangleright v],

(ii)

braided antisymmetry:

[u,v]=-[\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright v,\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright u],

(iii)

braided Jacobi identity:

[u,[v,w]]=[[u,v],w]+[\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright v,[\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright u,w]],

for all
u,v,w\in\mathfrak{g}
,
k\in K
.
A
K
-braided Lie
algebra morphism
\varphi:\mathfrak{g}\to\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}
is a morphism of the
K
-modules
\mathfrak{g}

and
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}
such that

\varphi\circ[\penalty\ ,\penalty\ ]=[\penalty\ ,\penalty\ ]^{\prime}\circ(\varphi\otimes\varphi)
, for
[\penalty\ ,\penalty\ ]
and
[\penalty\ ,\penalty\ ]^{\prime}
the brackets of
\mathfrak{g}
and
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}
respectively.

We add the subscript
[\penalty\ ,\penalty\ ]_{\mathsf{R}}
to the bracket when we need to
emphasize the
\mathsf{R}
-matrix structure we are using.

Lemma 5.2
.

Let
(K,\mathsf{R})
be a triangular Hopf algebra.
A
K
-module algebra
A
is a
K
-braided Lie algebra with bracket

(5.1)

[\penalty\ ,\penalty\ ]:A\otimes A\to A,\qquad a\otimes b\mapsto[a,b]=ab-(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright b)\,(\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright a)\,.

Proof.

The
K
-equivariance follows from the

K
-module algebra property (
2.4
) of
A
and quasi-cocommutativity of
K
:

\displaystyle k\vartriangleright[a,b]

\displaystyle=({k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\vartriangleright a)({k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\vartriangleright b)-({k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright b)({k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright a)

\displaystyle=({k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\vartriangleright a)({k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\vartriangleright b)-(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}{k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\vartriangleright b)(\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}{k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\vartriangleright a)

\displaystyle=[{k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\vartriangleright a,{k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\vartriangleright b]\,.

The braided antisymmetry follows from the triangularity of
K
:

\displaystyle[\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright b,\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright a]

\displaystyle=(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright b)(\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright a)-(\mathsf{R}_{\beta}\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright a)\,(\mathsf{R}^{\beta}\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright b)

\displaystyle=(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright b)(\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright a)-ab=-[a,b].

We are left to prove Jacobi identity. On the one hand, using
K
-equivariance of
[\,,\,]
just shown, and
condition
(\mathrm{id}\otimes\Delta)\mathsf{R}=\mathsf{R}_{13}\mathsf{R}_{12}
together with quasi co-commutativity, we have

\displaystyle[a,[b,c]]

\displaystyle=a[b,c]-(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright[b,c])(\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright a)

\displaystyle=a[b,c]-[{\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\vartriangleright b,{\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\vartriangleright c](\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright a)

\displaystyle=abc-a(\mathsf{R}_{\beta}\vartriangleright c)\,(\mathsf{R}^{\beta}\vartriangleright b)-({\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\vartriangleright b)({\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\vartriangleright c)(\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright a)

\displaystyle\quad+(\mathsf{R}_{\beta}{\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\vartriangleright c)(\mathsf{R}^{\beta}{\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\vartriangleright b)(\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright a)

\displaystyle=abc-a(\mathsf{R}_{\beta}\vartriangleright c)\,(\mathsf{R}^{\beta}\vartriangleright b)-({\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\vartriangleright b)({\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\vartriangleright c)(\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright a)

\displaystyle\quad+({\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\mathsf{R}_{\beta}\vartriangleright c)({\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\mathsf{R}^{\beta}\vartriangleright b)(\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright a)

\displaystyle=abc-a(\mathsf{R}_{\beta}\vartriangleright c)\,(\mathsf{R}^{\beta}\vartriangleright b)-(\mathsf{R}_{\gamma}\vartriangleright b)(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright c)(\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\mathsf{R}^{\gamma}\vartriangleright a)

\displaystyle\quad+(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\mathsf{R}_{\beta}\vartriangleright c)\,(\mathsf{R}_{\gamma}\mathsf{R}^{\beta}\vartriangleright b)(\mathsf{R}^{\gamma}\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright a)\,.

On the other hand, using also
(\Delta\otimes\mathrm{id})\mathsf{R}=\mathsf{R}_{13}\mathsf{R}_{23}
, analogous computations give

\displaystyle[[a,b],c]+[\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright b,[\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright a,c]]

\displaystyle=[a,b]c-(\mathsf{R}_{\beta}\vartriangleright c)\,(\mathsf{R}^{\beta}\vartriangleright[a,b])+(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright b)[\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright a,c]-(\mathsf{R}_{\beta}\vartriangleright[\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright a,c])(\mathsf{R}^{\beta}\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright b)

\displaystyle=[a,b]c-(\mathsf{R}_{\beta}\vartriangleright c)\,[{\mathsf{R}^{\beta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\vartriangleright a,{\mathsf{R}^{\beta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\vartriangleright b]+(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright b)[\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright a,c]

\displaystyle\quad-[{\mathsf{R}_{\beta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright a,{\mathsf{R}_{\beta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\vartriangleright c](\mathsf{R}^{\beta}\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright b)

\displaystyle=[a,b]c-(\mathsf{R}_{\gamma}\mathsf{R}_{\beta}\vartriangleright c)\,[\mathsf{R}^{\gamma}\vartriangleright a,\mathsf{R}^{\beta}\vartriangleright b]+(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright b)[\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright a,c]

\displaystyle\quad-[\mathsf{R}_{\gamma}\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright a,\mathsf{R}_{\beta}\vartriangleright c](\mathsf{R}^{\beta}\mathsf{R}^{\gamma}\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright b)

\displaystyle=[a,b]c-(\mathsf{R}_{\gamma}\mathsf{R}_{\beta}\vartriangleright c)\,[\mathsf{R}^{\gamma}\vartriangleright a,\mathsf{R}^{\beta}\vartriangleright b]+(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright b)[\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright a,c]-[a,\mathsf{R}_{\beta}\vartriangleright c](\mathsf{R}^{\beta}\vartriangleright b)

\displaystyle=abc-(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright b)\,(\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright a)c-(\mathsf{R}_{\gamma}\mathsf{R}_{\beta}\vartriangleright c)\,(\mathsf{R}^{\gamma}\vartriangleright a)(\mathsf{R}^{\beta}\vartriangleright b)

\displaystyle\quad+(\mathsf{R}_{\gamma}\mathsf{R}_{\beta}\vartriangleright c)(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\mathsf{R}^{\beta}\vartriangleright b)\,(\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\mathsf{R}^{\gamma}\vartriangleright a)+(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright b)(\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright a)c

\displaystyle\quad-(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright b)(\mathsf{R}_{\beta}\vartriangleright c)\,(\mathsf{R}^{\beta}\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright a)-a(\mathsf{R}_{\beta}\vartriangleright c)(\mathsf{R}^{\beta}\vartriangleright b)+(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\mathsf{R}_{\beta}\vartriangleright c)(\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright a)(\mathsf{R}^{\beta}\vartriangleright b)

\displaystyle=abc+(\mathsf{R}_{\gamma}\mathsf{R}_{\beta}\vartriangleright c)(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\mathsf{R}^{\beta}\vartriangleright b)\,(\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\mathsf{R}^{\gamma}\vartriangleright a)

\displaystyle\quad-(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright b)(\mathsf{R}_{\beta}\vartriangleright c)\,(\mathsf{R}^{\beta}\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright a)-a(\mathsf{R}_{\beta}\vartriangleright c)(\mathsf{R}^{\beta}\vartriangleright b)

where for the fourth equality we have used the triangularity of
\mathsf{R}
.
This expression equals that for
[a,[b,c]]
found above.
∎

Remark 5.3
.

Let
(K,\mathsf{R})
be a triangular Hopf algebra. A
K
-braided Poisson
algebra

(P,\cdot,[\penalty\ ,\penalty\ ])
is a
K
-module algebra
(P,\cdot)
,
and a

K
-braided Lie algebra
(P,[\penalty\ ,\penalty\ ])
such that the bracket is a braided derivation of the multiplication: for all
p,r,s\in P
,

(5.2)

[p,r\cdot s]=[p,r]\cdot s+\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright r\cdot[\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright p,s]\penalty\ .

In particular, any
K
-braided algebra
(A,\cdot)
is canonically a
K
-braided Poisson
algebra
(A,\cdot,[\penalty\ ,\penalty\ ])
with the bracket
defined in (
5.1
).

An enveloping algebra of
\mathfrak{g}
is the datum
(A,\alpha)
of a

K
-module algebra
A
, with braided Lie algebra structure as in Lemma
5.2
,
and
a braided Lie algebra morphism
\alpha:\mathfrak{g}\to A
, that is a
K
-equivariant map such that

\alpha([u,v])=[\alpha(u),\alpha(v)]
, for all
u,v\in\mathfrak{g}
.

The universal enveloping algebra
(\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g}),\iota)
of
\mathfrak{g}
is characterised by the following universal
property: given an enveloping algebra
(A,\alpha)
there is a
unique lift of
\alpha
to a
K
-equivariant algebra map
\hat{\alpha}:\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g})\to A
, such that
\hat{\alpha}\circ\iota=\alpha:\mathfrak{g}\to A
.

Existence of
(\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g}),\iota)
follows from considering the usual
tensor algebra
T(\mathfrak{g})
, canonically a
K
-module algebra, and the quotient
\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g}):=T(\mathfrak{g})/I

where
I
is the algebra ideal generated by
u\otimes v-\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright v\otimes\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright u-[u,v]
. The quotient is a
K
-module algebra since the ideal
I
is preserved by the
K
-action due to the quasi-cocommutativity of
K
. The
K
-equivariant map
\hat{\iota}:\mathfrak{g}\to T(\mathfrak{g})
,
u\mapsto\hat{\iota}(u)=u

induces the braided Lie algebra morphism
\iota:\mathfrak{g}\to\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g})
on the quotient. As in the classical case, the universality
of
(\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g}),\iota)
follows from that of
T(\mathfrak{g})
and uniqueness (up to isomorphism) from the universal property.

Proposition 5.4
.

The universal enveloping algebra
\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g})
of a braided Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
is a braided Hopf
algebra associated with the triangular Hopf algebra
(K,\mathsf{R})
.

Proof.

We first show that the tensor algebra
T(\mathfrak{g})
is a braided Hopf algebra.
We define the coproduct
\Delta(u)=u\boxtimes 1+1\boxtimes u
for degree one elements
u\in\mathfrak{g}
and extend it to all

T(\mathfrak{g})
by requiring it to be a unital algebra map between
T(\mathfrak{g})
and the braided tensor
algebra
T(\mathfrak{g})\boxtimes T(\mathfrak{g})
(cf. Proposition
3.3
):

\Delta(\zeta\otimes\xi)=({\zeta}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\otimes\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}})\boxtimes(\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright{\zeta}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\otimes{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}})

for all
\zeta,\xi\in T(\mathfrak{g})
.
Coassociativity of
\Delta
on degree one elements is trivial, it is then easy to see that if it holds for
elements
\zeta
and
\xi
in
T(\mathfrak{g})
it holds also for their product.
The counit
\varepsilon:T(\mathfrak{g})\to\mathds{k}
is the unital algebra map defined by

\varepsilon(u)=0
for all
u\in\mathfrak{g}
.
Then
(T(\mathfrak{g}),\Delta,\varepsilon)

is a
K
-module coalgebra as in (
2.6
).
Indeed,
the
K
-module coalgebra property
\Delta(k\vartriangleright\zeta)={k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\vartriangleright{\zeta}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\boxtimes{k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\vartriangleright{\zeta}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}

holds on degree one elements and, using quasi-cocommutativity of
K
, it is seen to hold on the
product
\zeta\otimes\xi
if it holds on the single elements
\zeta,\xi\in T(\mathfrak{g})
. The condition on the counit is trivially satisfied. Since
\Delta
has been defined as an algebra map

T(\mathfrak{g})\to T(\mathfrak{g})\boxtimes T(\mathfrak{g})
we have that
T(\mathfrak{g})
is a braided bialgebra
associated with
(K,\mathsf{R})
. It is a braided Hopf algebra with antipode
the
K
-equivariant map
S(u)=-u
for
u\in\mathfrak{g}
, extended to
T(\mathfrak{g})
as braided anti-algebra map.

Finally, the quotient
\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g})=T(\mathfrak{g})/I
is a braided Hopf algebra since the ideal

I=\langle u\otimes v-\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright v\otimes\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright u-[u,v]\rangle
is a braided Hopf
ideal. Indeed, as already mentioned
I
is a
K
-module; it is also a coideal since

\Delta(u\otimes v-\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright v\otimes\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright v-[u,v])\in I\boxtimes T(\mathfrak{g})+T(\mathfrak{g})\boxtimes I

for all
u,v\in\mathfrak{g}
, as can be shown by using triangularity of the universal
\mathsf{R}
matrix.
∎

Remark 5.5
.

Over a field of characteristic zero a braided Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
is
the
K
-submodule in
\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g})
of primitive elements:

{\rm{Prim}}(\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g}))=\mathfrak{g}
. More in general, a connected braided cocommutative
Hopf algebra
L
is the universal enveloping algebra of the braided Lie
algebra
\mathfrak{g}={\rm{Prim}}(L)
of its primitive elements, see
[
24
]
.

5.2.
Braided Lie algebras of braided derivations

We now study derivations of
K
-module algebras.

Let
A
be a
K
-module algebra and
({\rm{Hom}}(A,A),\vartriangleright_{{\rm{Hom}}(A,A)})
the
K
-module
of linear maps from
A
to
A
as in (
2.1
).
We denote

(5.3)

\mathrm{Der}{(A)}:=\{\psi\in{\rm{Hom}}(A,A)\,|\,\psi(aa^{\prime})=\psi(a)a^{\prime}+(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright a)\,(\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright_{{\rm{Hom}}(A,A)}\psi)(a^{\prime})\}

the
\mathds{k}
-module
of
braided derivations
of
A
. We add the superscript
\mathsf{R}

and write
\rm{Der}^{\mathsf{R}}{(A)}

when we wish to emphasize the
role of the braiding.
Braided derivations are
a
K
-submodule of
{\rm{Hom}}(A,A)
:

Lemma 5.6
.

For
(K,\mathsf{R})
quasi-triangular,

\mathrm{Der}{(A)}
is a
K
-module with action given by the restriction of

\vartriangleright_{{\rm{Hom}}(A,A)}
:

\displaystyle\vartriangleright_{\mathrm{Der}{(A)}}:K\otimes\mathrm{Der}{(A)}

\displaystyle\to\mathrm{Der}{(A)}

(5.4)

\displaystyle k\otimes\psi

\displaystyle\mapsto k\vartriangleright_{\mathrm{Der}{(A)}}\psi:\;a\mapsto{k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\vartriangleright\psi(S({k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}})\vartriangleright a).

Proof.

We only need to show that the restriction of
\vartriangleright_{{\rm{Hom}}(A,A)}
to
\mathrm{Der}{(A)}
has image in
\mathrm{Der}{(A)}
, that is,
k\vartriangleright_{{\rm{Hom}}(A,A)}\psi
is a braided derivation if
\psi
is such, for each
k\in K
.

\displaystyle(k\vartriangleright

\displaystyle\psi)(ab)={k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\vartriangleright\psi\big(S({k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}})\vartriangleright(ab)\big)

\displaystyle={k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\vartriangleright\psi\big((S({k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(3)}})\vartriangleright a)(S({k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}})\vartriangleright b)\big)

\displaystyle={k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\vartriangleright\Big(\psi\big(S({k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(3)}})\vartriangleright a\big)(S({k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}})\vartriangleright b)+(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}S({k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(3)}})\vartriangleright a)(\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright\psi)\big(S({k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}})\vartriangleright b\big)\Big)

\displaystyle={k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\vartriangleright\psi\big(S({k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(4)}})\vartriangleright a\big)\;({k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}S({k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(3)}})\vartriangleright b)

\displaystyle\qquad+({k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}S({k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(4)}})\vartriangleright a)\;{k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\vartriangleright\big((\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright\psi)(S({k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(3)}})\vartriangleright b)\big)

using that
\psi
is a braided derivation and
A
is a module algebra.
Then, the first term in the sum simplifies to
(k\vartriangleright\psi)(a)\,b
, while for the second term we have

\displaystyle\big({k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}S({k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(4)}})\vartriangleright a\big)\;

\displaystyle{k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\vartriangleright\big((\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright\psi)(S({k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(3)}})\vartriangleright b)\big)

\displaystyle=({k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}S({k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(4)}})\vartriangleright a)\;({k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}{\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}})\vartriangleright\psi(S({k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(3)}}{\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}})\vartriangleright b)

\displaystyle=({k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\mathsf{R}_{\beta}S({k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(4)}})\vartriangleright a)\;({k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\mathsf{R}^{\alpha})\vartriangleright\psi(S({k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(3)}}\mathsf{R}^{\beta}))\vartriangleright b)

\displaystyle=({k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}S({k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(4)}}{\overline{\mathsf{R}}}_{\beta})\vartriangleright a)\;({k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\mathsf{R}^{\alpha})\vartriangleright\psi(S({k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(3)}}{\overline{\mathsf{R}}}^{\beta})\vartriangleright b)

\displaystyle=(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}{k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}S({\overline{\mathsf{R}}}_{\beta}{k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(3)}})\vartriangleright a)\;(\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}{k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}})\vartriangleright\psi(S({\overline{\mathsf{R}}}^{\beta}{k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(4)}})\vartriangleright b)

\displaystyle=(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\mathsf{R}_{\beta}\vartriangleright a)\;(\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}{k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}})\vartriangleright\psi(S(\mathsf{R}^{\beta}{k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}})\vartriangleright b)

using quasitriangularity for the second equality,
(\mathrm{id}\otimes S){\overline{\mathsf{R}}}=\mathsf{R}
for the third one, and
quasi-cocommutativity twice. Finally, again by quasitriangularity this is rewritten as

(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright a)\;({(\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}k)}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}})\vartriangleright\psi(S({(\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}))\vartriangleright b)=(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright a)\;((\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}k)\vartriangleright\psi)(b)

thus showing that
k\vartriangleright\psi
is a braided derivation:

(k\vartriangleright\psi)(ab)=(k\vartriangleright\psi)(a)b+(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright a)\;(\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright(k\vartriangleright\psi))(b).

This ends the proof.
∎

As in Lemma
5.2
, when the Hopf algebra
K
is triangular, associated with the
K
-module
algebra
({\rm{Hom}}(A,A),\circ)
there is the braided Lie algebra

({\rm{Hom}}(A,A),[\penalty\ ,\penalty\ ])
where
[\penalty\ ,\penalty\ ]
is the braided commutator as in (
5.1
).

For
a\in A
we define

(5.5)

\ell_{a}\in{\rm{Hom}}(A,A)\penalty\ ,\penalty\ \penalty\ \ell_{a}:A\to A\,,\penalty\ a^{\prime}\mapsto\ell_{a}(a^{\prime})=aa^{\prime}\penalty\ .

Using the
K
-module structure (
2.1
) of
{\rm{Hom}}(A,A)
, one shows that
\ell:A\to{\rm{Hom}}(A,A)
,
a\mapsto\ell_{a}
, is
K
-equivariant,
k\vartriangleright\ell_{a}=\ell_{k\vartriangleright a}
for all
k\in K
,
a\in A
. It follows that a linear map
\psi\in{\rm{Hom}}(A,A)
is a
(K,\mathsf{R})
-braided derivation,
\psi(ab)=\psi(a)b+(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright a)\,(\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright\psi)(b)
, if and only if

\psi(\ell_{a}(b))=\ell_{\psi(a)}(b)+(\ell_{\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright a}\circ(\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright\psi))(b)
, that is
if and only if

(5.6)

[\psi,\ell_{a}]=\ell_{\psi(a)}

for all
a\in A
.

Proposition 5.7
.

Let
(K,\mathsf{R})
be triangular.
For each
K
-module algebra
A
, the
K
-module of braided
derivations with

\displaystyle[\penalty\ ,\penalty\ ]:

\displaystyle\,\mathrm{Der}{(A)}\otimes\mathrm{Der}{(A)}\to\mathrm{Der}{(A)}

(5.7)

\displaystyle\psi\otimes\lambda\mapsto[\psi,\lambda]:=\psi\circ\lambda-(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright_{\mathrm{Der}{(A)}}\lambda)\circ(\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright_{\mathrm{Der}{(A)}}\psi)

is a braided Lie subalgebra of
{\rm{Hom}}(A,A)
.

Proof.

We only need to show that the image of
[\penalty\ ,\penalty\ ]
is in
\mathrm{Der}{(A)}
.
Let
\psi,\lambda\in\mathrm{Der}{(A)}
and
a\in A
. Using the
characterization (
5.6
) of briaded derivations and the Jacobi identity in
{\rm{Hom}}(A,A)
, we have

\displaystyle[[\psi,\lambda],\ell_{a}]

\displaystyle=[\psi,[\lambda,\ell_{a}]]-[\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright\lambda,[\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright\psi,\ell_{a}]]

\displaystyle=[\psi,\ell_{\lambda(a)}]-[\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright\lambda,\ell_{(\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright\psi)(a)}]

\displaystyle=\ell_{\psi(\lambda(a))}-\ell_{(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright\lambda)(\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright\psi)(a)}

\displaystyle=\ell_{[\psi,\lambda](a)}

and thus
[\psi,\lambda]
is a braided derivation.
∎

Recall that the
K
-module algebra
A
is
quasi-commutative
(or
braided commutative) when

(5.8)

a\,a^{\prime}=(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright{a^{\prime}})\,(\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright{a})\penalty\ ,

for all
a,a^{\prime}\in A
. In this case the braided Lie algebra
\mathrm{Der}{(A)}
is also
a left
A
-submodule of
{\rm{Hom}}(A,A)

by defining

(5.9)

(a\psi)(c):=a\,\psi(c)

for
\psi\in{\rm{Hom}}(A,A)
,
a,c\in A
, that is,

a\psi:=\ell_{a}\circ\psi\penalty\ .

Indeed, for
a\in A
,

\psi\in\mathrm{Der}{(A)}
one has:

\displaystyle a\psi(cc^{\prime})

\displaystyle=a\psi(c)c^{\prime}+a(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright c)(\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright\psi)(c^{\prime})=a\psi(c)c^{\prime}+(\mathsf{R}_{\beta}\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright c)(\mathsf{R}^{\beta}\vartriangleright a)(\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright\psi)(c^{\prime})

\displaystyle=a\psi(c)c^{\prime}+(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright c)({\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\vartriangleright a)({\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\vartriangleright\psi)(c^{\prime})=a\psi(c)c^{\prime}+(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright c)(\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright a\psi)(c^{\prime})\,.

The compatibility with the
K
-action, as in (
2.5
),
shows that
\mathrm{Der}{(A)}
is a
(K,A)
-relative Hopf module. The compatibility of the
A
-module and the braided
Lie algebra structures follows from

({\rm{Hom}}(A,A),\circ,[\penalty\ ,\penalty\ ])
being a braided Poisson algebra (cf. Remark
5.3
).

Proposition 5.8
.

Let
(K,\mathsf{R})
be triangular and let
A
be a quasi-commutative
K
-module
algebra. The Lie bracket of
\mathrm{Der}{(A)}
is a derivation of the

A
-module
\mathrm{Der}{(A)}
, that is, it satisfies, for all

a,a^{\prime}\in A,\psi,\psi^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Der}{(A)}
,

[\psi,a^{\prime}\psi^{\prime}]=\psi(a^{\prime})\psi^{\prime}+\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright a^{\prime}[\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright\psi,\psi^{\prime}]

and more generally

(5.10)

\begin{split}[a\psi,a^{\prime}\psi^{\prime}]\,=&\penalty\ a\psi(a^{\prime})\psi^{\prime}+a(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright a^{\prime})[\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright\psi,\psi^{\prime}]\\[1.99997pt]
&+\mathsf{R}_{\beta}\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright a^{\prime}\big(\mathsf{R}_{\delta}\mathsf{R}_{\gamma}\vartriangleright\psi^{\prime}\big)\!\!\>(\mathsf{R}^{\delta}\mathsf{R}^{\beta}\vartriangleright a)\>\!\mathsf{R}^{\gamma}\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright\psi\penalty\ .\end{split}

Remark 5.9
.

The knowledge of the bracket on a
K
-submodule
\mathfrak{X}
of

\mathrm{Der}{(A)}
(not necessarily a braided Lie subalgebra)
that generates
\mathrm{Der}{(A)}
as a left
A
-module is therefore sufficient
to determine via (
5.10
) the

K
-braided Lie algebra
\mathrm{Der}{(A)}
.

Proof.

Let
(P,\cdot,[\penalty\ ,\penalty\ ])
be a
K
-braided Poisson algebra as in Remark

5.3
. From (
5.2
) and the braided antisymmetry of the bracket we have,
[p\cdot q,r]=p\cdot[q,r]+[p,\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright r]\cdot\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright q
, for
all
p,q,r\in P
. Using
this and (
5.2
) again we obtain
more generally

(5.11)

\begin{split}[p\cdot q,p^{\prime}\cdot q^{\prime}]\,=&\penalty\ p\cdot[q,p^{\prime}]\cdot q^{\prime}+[p,\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright p^{\prime}]\cdot\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright q\cdot q^{\prime}+\mathsf{R}_{\beta}\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright p^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{R}^{\beta}\vartriangleright p\cdot[\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright q,q^{\prime}]\\
&+\mathsf{R}_{\beta}\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright p^{\prime}\cdot[\mathsf{R}^{\beta}\vartriangleright p,\mathsf{R}_{\gamma}\vartriangleright q^{\prime}]\cdot\mathsf{R}^{\gamma}\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright q\penalty\ .\\[-11.99998pt]
\end{split}

We now consider the case
(P,\cdot,[\penalty\ ,\penalty\ ])=({\rm{Hom}}(A,A),\circ,[\penalty\ ,\penalty\ ])
.
When
A
is quasi-commutative the left action of
A
on
\mathrm{Der}{(A)}

is
a\psi=\ell_{a}\circ\psi
for all
a\in A
,
\psi\in\mathrm{Der}{(A)}
, so
that
[\psi,\ell_{a^{\prime}}]\circ\psi^{\prime}=\ell_{\psi(a^{\prime})}\circ\psi^{\prime}=\psi(a^{\prime})\psi^{\prime}
. Quasi-commutativity of
A
in the form

[\ell_{a},\ell_{a^{\prime}}]=0
for all
a,a^{\prime}\in A
implies

[\ell_{a},\ell_{\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright a^{\prime}}]\otimes\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}=0
. Then, one obtains
equation (
5.10
) setting
p=\ell_{a}
,
p^{\prime}=\ell_{a^{\prime}}
,
q=\psi
,

q^{\prime}=\psi^{\prime}
in (
5.11
).
∎

Example 5.10
.

Braided Lie algebra of a bicovariant
differential calculus on a cotriangular Hopf algebra.

Let
(A,R)
be a cotriangular Hopf algebra with dual triangular Hopf
algebra
(U,\mathscr{R})
, with
pairing
\langle\,,\,\rangle:U\otimes A\to\mathds{k}
such that
R(a\otimes a^{\prime})=\langle\mathscr{R},a\otimes a^{\prime}\rangle:=\langle\mathscr{R}^{\alpha},a\rangle\langle\mathscr{R}_{\alpha},a^{\prime}\rangle
,
for all
a,a^{\prime}\in A
. The opposite Hopf algebra
U^{op}
is triangular with universal matrix
\overline{\mathscr{R}}
.
The algebra
A
is a
U^{op}\otimes U
-module algebra via

\vartriangleright:U^{op}\otimes U\otimes A\to A
,
(\zeta\otimes\xi)\vartriangleright a=\langle\zeta,{a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\rangle{a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\langle\xi,{a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(3)}}\rangle
.
Furthermore,

U^{op}\otimes U
is triangular with

\mathfrak{R}=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\rm{flip}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\overline{\mathscr{R}}\otimes\mathscr{R})\penalty\ .

It is not difficult to see that the
property
m_{op}=R*m*\overline{R}
of the
cotriangular Hopf algebra
(A,R)
(see (ii) in Definition
3.11
) is just
the quasi-commutativity property
(
5.8
) of
A
with respect to the triangular structure
\mathfrak{R}
of
U^{op}\otimes U
. In this quasi-commutative case
the canonical notion of braided derivations of
A

is given by equation (
5.3
) where the braiding is the one
associated with
{\mathfrak{R}}
; we denote this braided Lie algebra by

{\rm{Der}}^{\mathfrak{R}\!\!\>}(A)
.
Hence

{\rm{Der}}^{\mathfrak{R}\!\!\>}(A)
is a
U^{op}\otimes U
-braided
Lie algebra and a
(U^{op}\otimes U,A)
-relative Hopf
module, the compatibility between these structures being as in
Proposition
5.8
.

As shown in
[
1
, §3.3]
, if
U
separates the elements
of
A
, the braided derivations

{\rm{Der}}^{\mathfrak{R}\!\!\>}(A)
define a bicovariant differential calculus on
A
,
the unique bicovariant differential calculus compatible with the
cotriangular structure
R
studied in
[
21
, §4.3]
.
The braided derivations

{\rm{Der}}^{\mathfrak{R}\!\!\>}(A)
give the
A
-bicovariant bimodule dual
to that of one-forms.

By the fundamental theorem of bicovariant bimodules

{\rm{Der}}^{\mathfrak{R}\!\!\>}(A)=A\otimes{\rm{Der}}^{\mathfrak{R}\!\!\>}(A){}_{\rm{inv}}
,
that is, braided derivations
are freely generated as a left

A
-module by the right-invariant ones

(5.12)

{\rm{Der}}^{\mathfrak{R}\!\!\>}(A){}_{\rm{inv}}:=\{u\in{\rm{Der}}^{\mathfrak{R}\!\!\>}(A)\,|\,\Delta(u(a))=u({a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}})\otimes{a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\mbox{ for all }a\in A\}\,.

We write
{\rm{Der}}^{\mathfrak{R}\!\!\>}(A){}_{\rm{inv}}

rather than
{\rm{Der}}^{\mathfrak{R}\!\!\>}_{\mathcal{M}^{A}}(A){}

to conform with Woronowicz’s notation
[
36
]
. Right-invariance implies
that the
U
-action is trivial,

\xi\vartriangleright u=\varepsilon(\xi)u\>\!

for all
\xi\in U
,
u\in{\rm{Der}}^{\mathfrak{R}\!\!\>}(A){}_{\rm{inv}}
. Indeed

\xi\vartriangleright(u(a))=u(a)_{(1)}\langle\xi,u(a)_{(2)}\rangle=u(a_{(1)})\langle\xi,a_{(2)}\rangle=u(\xi\vartriangleright a)
. Also the
U^{op}
-action closes in

{\rm{Der}}^{\mathfrak{R}\!\!\>}(A){}_{\rm{inv}}
:

(5.13)

\begin{split}(\zeta\vartriangleright u)(a)&={\zeta}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\vartriangleright(u(S^{-1}({\zeta}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}})\vartriangleright a))\\
&=\langle S^{-1}({\zeta}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}),{a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\rangle\,{\zeta}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\vartriangleright(u({a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}))\\
&=\langle S^{-1}({\zeta}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}),{a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\rangle\langle{\zeta}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}},{u({a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}})}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\rangle\,{u({a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}})}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\\
&=\langle S^{-1}({\zeta}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}),{a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\rangle\langle{\zeta}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}},u({a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}})\rangle\,{a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(3)}}\end{split}

for all
\zeta\in U^{op}
,
u\in{\rm{Der}}^{\mathfrak{R}\!\!\>}(A){}_{\rm{inv}}
,
a\in A
,
and

(5.14)

\Delta((\zeta\vartriangleright u)(a))=\langle S^{-1}({\zeta}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}),{a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\rangle\langle{\zeta}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}},{u({a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}})}\rangle\,{a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(3)}}\otimes{a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(4)}}=(\zeta\vartriangleright u)({a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}})\otimes{a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\penalty\ .

This implies that
{\rm{Der}}^{\mathfrak{R}\!\!\>}(A){}_{\rm{inv}}
is
a
U^{op}\otimes U
-submodule and a braided subalgebra of

{\rm{Der}}^{\mathfrak{R}\!\!\>}(A){}
. Since

(\mathfrak{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright a)\!\>\mathfrak{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright u=(\overline{\mathscr{R}}_{\alpha}\!\!\>\vartriangleright\!\!\>(\mathscr{R}_{\beta}\vartriangleright a))\;\!\overline{\mathscr{R}}^{\alpha}\!\!\>\vartriangleright\!\!\>(\mathscr{R}^{\beta}\vartriangleright u)=(\overline{\mathscr{R}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright a)\>\!\overline{\mathscr{R}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright u\penalty\ ,

we further have

(5.15)

{\rm{Der}}^{\mathfrak{R}\!\!\>}(A){}_{\rm{inv}}={\rm{Der}}^{\overline{\mathscr{R}}\!\!\>}(A){}_{\rm{inv}}\penalty\ .

This is a
U^{op}
-braided Lie algebra
isomorphism.

\blacksquare

5.3.
Actions of braided Lie algebras and universal enveloping algebras

An action of a
K
-braided Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
on a
K
-module
W
is a

K
-equivariant map

{{\mathcal{L}}}:\mathfrak{g}\otimes W\to W\penalty\ ,\penalty\ \penalty\ u\otimes w\mapsto{{\mathcal{L}}}_{u}(w)

such that

{{\mathcal{L}}}_{[u,v]}={{\mathcal{L}}}_{u}\circ{{\mathcal{L}}}_{v}-{{\mathcal{L}}}_{\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright_{V}v}\circ{{\mathcal{L}}}_{\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright_{V}u}

for all
u,v\in\mathfrak{g}
.
Equivalently, an action such that

({\rm{Hom}}(W,W),{\mathcal{L}})
is an enveloping algebra of
\mathfrak{g}
.

When
W=A
is a
K
-module algebra, the action of
\mathfrak{g}

is in addition required to satisfy the braided Leibniz rule:

(5.16)

{\mathcal{L}}_{u}(ab)={\mathcal{L}}_{u}(a)b+(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright a)\,{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright_{\mathfrak{g}}u}(b)\penalty\ ,

for all

u\in\mathfrak{g}
,
a,b\in A
.
Equivalently,
{\mathcal{L}}:\mathfrak{g}\to{\rm{Hom}}(A,A)
is required to have image in
\mathrm{Der}{(A)}
. Hence it is a morphism

{\mathcal{L}}:\mathfrak{g}\to\mathrm{Der}{(A)}
of braided Lie algebras.

Proposition 5.11
.

Let
\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g})
be the universal enveloping algebra of a braided Lie
algebra
\mathfrak{g}
associated with
(K,\mathsf{R})
.
Any action
{\mathcal{L}}:\mathfrak{g}\otimes A\to A
of a braided Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
on a
K
-module
algebra
A
lifts to an action
{\mathcal{L}}:\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g})\otimes A\to A
of the braided Hopf algebra
\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g})

on
A
.

Proof.

By definition of universal enveloping algebra,
the
K
-braided Lie algebra morphism
{\mathcal{L}}:(\mathfrak{g},[\penalty\ ,\penalty\ ])\to({\rm{Hom}}(A,A),[\penalty\ ,\penalty\ ])
lifts
to a unique
K
-equivariant algebra morphism
\widehat{{\mathcal{L}}}:(\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g}),\cdot)\to({\rm{Hom}}(A,A),\circ)
that with
slight abuse of notation we still denote
{{\mathcal{L}}}:\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g})\to{\rm{Hom}}(A,A)
.

We show that condition (
3.17
) holds; for all

\xi\in\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g})
,

(5.17)

{\mathcal{L}}_{\xi}(ab)={\mathcal{L}}_{{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}}(\mathsf{R}_{\gamma}\vartriangleright a)\,{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathsf{R}^{\gamma}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}}(b)

(where we dropped the subscripts of the
K
-actions).
This is indeed the case if
\xi\in\mathfrak{g}\subset\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g})
. The equality is
then proven inductively, by showing that if
\zeta,\xi\in\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g})
satisfy (
5.17
)
then so does the product
\zeta\xi
. On one hand we have

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{\zeta\xi}(ab)={\mathcal{L}}_{\zeta}({\mathcal{L}}_{\xi}(ab))

\displaystyle={\mathcal{L}}_{\zeta}({\mathcal{L}}_{{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}}(\mathsf{R}_{\gamma}\vartriangleright a)\!\>{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathsf{R}^{\gamma}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}}(b))

\displaystyle={\mathcal{L}}_{{\zeta}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}}(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright{\mathcal{L}}_{{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}}(\mathsf{R}_{\gamma}\vartriangleright a))\!\>{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright{\zeta}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}}({\mathcal{L}}_{\mathsf{R}^{\gamma}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}}(b))

\displaystyle={\mathcal{L}}_{{\zeta}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}}({\mathcal{L}}_{\mathsf{R}_{{\alpha}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}}(\mathsf{R}_{{\alpha}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}}\mathsf{R}_{\gamma}\vartriangleright a))\>\!{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright{\zeta}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}}({\mathcal{L}}_{\mathsf{R}^{\gamma}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}}(b))

\displaystyle={\mathcal{L}}_{{\zeta}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}}{\mathcal{L}}_{{\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}}(\mathsf{R}_{\beta}\mathsf{R}_{\gamma}\vartriangleright a)\;\!{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathsf{R}^{\beta}\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright{\zeta}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}}({\mathcal{L}}_{\mathsf{R}^{\gamma}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}}(b))

\displaystyle={\mathcal{L}}_{{\zeta}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}})}(\mathsf{R}_{\beta}\mathsf{R}_{\gamma}\vartriangleright a)\>\!{\mathcal{L}}_{(\mathsf{R}^{\beta}\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright{\zeta}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}})(\mathsf{R}^{\gamma}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}})}(b)\penalty\ .

On the other hand, since

\Delta(\zeta\xi)=\Delta(\zeta)\,{\raisebox{1.20554pt}{\rule{2.58334pt}{2.58334pt}}}\,\Delta(\xi)={\zeta}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}})\otimes(\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright{\zeta}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}){\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{{(\zeta\xi)}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}}(\mathsf{R}_{\gamma}\vartriangleright a){\mathcal{L}}_{\mathsf{R}^{\gamma}\vartriangleright{(\zeta\xi)}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}}(b)\,

\displaystyle={\mathcal{L}}_{{\zeta}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}})}(\mathsf{R}_{\gamma}\vartriangleright a)\>\!{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathsf{R}^{\gamma}\vartriangleright((\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright{\zeta}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}){\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}})}(b)

\displaystyle={\mathcal{L}}_{{\zeta}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}})}(\mathsf{R}_{\gamma}\vartriangleright a)\>\!{\mathcal{L}}_{{(\mathsf{R}^{\gamma}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright{\zeta}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}})({\mathsf{R}^{\gamma}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}})}(b)

\displaystyle={\mathcal{L}}_{{\zeta}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}})}(\mathsf{R}_{\beta}\mathsf{R}_{\gamma}\vartriangleright a)\>\!{\mathcal{L}}_{(\mathsf{R}^{\beta}\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright{\zeta}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}})(\mathsf{R}^{\gamma}\vartriangleright{\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}})}(b)\penalty\ .

Comparing these two
expressions we obtain
