{\mathcal{L}}_{\zeta\xi}(ab)={\mathcal{L}}_{{(\zeta\xi)}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}}(\mathsf{R}_{\gamma}\vartriangleright a)\>\!{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathsf{R}^{\gamma}\vartriangleright{(\zeta\xi)}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}}(b)\,
.
∎

When
\mathfrak{g}=\mathrm{Der}{(A)}
with action
\mathrm{Der}{(A)}\otimes A\to A
given by the evaluation, we obtain:

Corollary 5.12
.

Let
A
be a
K
-module algebra and
\mathrm{Der}{(A)}
the
associated braided Lie algebra of derivations.
The universal enveloping algebra
\mathcal{U}(\mathrm{Der}{(A)})
acts on
A
as a

K
-braided Hopf algebra.

Thus, a
K
-module algebra
A
is canonically a braided

\mathcal{U}(\mathrm{Der}{(A)})
-module algebra with respect to the braided Hopf algebra
\mathcal{U}(\mathrm{Der}{(A)})
.
We notice that in this context primitive
elements
\psi\in\mathrm{Der}{(A)}\subset\mathcal{U}(\mathrm{Der}{(A)})
act on
A
as braided derivations:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{\psi}(ab)={\mathcal{L}}_{{\psi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}}(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright_{A}a)\,(\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright_{\mathrm{Der}{(A)}}{\mathcal{L}}_{{\psi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}})(b)={\mathcal{L}}_{\psi}(a)b+(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright a)\,{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright_{\mathrm{Der}{(A)}}\psi}(b).

There is a canonical action of a braided Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
on the

K
-module algebra
\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g})
, the adjoint action.

Proposition 5.13
.

Given a braided Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
associated with
(K,\mathsf{R})
, the braided adjoint action
(
3.18
) is an action of
\mathfrak{g}
on the braided Hopf algebra
\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g})
; equivalently, a morphism
\mathfrak{g}\to\mathrm{Der}{(\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g}))}
of braided Lie algebras.

Proof.

Let
\mathfrak{g}
be a braided Lie algebra and
\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g})
its universal enveloping algebra, a braided Hopf algebra for
Proposition
5.4
.
From Lemma
3.9
, there is a
morphism of
K
-modules:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}:(\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g}),\vartriangleright_{\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g})})

\displaystyle\to({\rm{Hom}}(\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g}),\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g})),\vartriangleright_{{\rm{Hom}}(\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g}),\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g}))})

\displaystyle\xi

\displaystyle\mapsto{\mathcal{L}}_{\xi},\quad{\mathcal{L}}_{\xi}(\zeta):=\xi\blacktriangleright_{\mathsf{ad}_{\mathsf{R}}}\zeta={\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright\zeta)\,\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright S_{\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g})}({\xi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}),\quad\zeta\in\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g}).

This restricts to the
K
-module morphism

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}:(\mathfrak{g},\vartriangleright_{\mathfrak{g}})

\displaystyle\to(\mathrm{Der}{(\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g}))},\vartriangleright_{\mathrm{Der}{(\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g}))}})

\displaystyle u

\displaystyle\mapsto{\mathcal{L}}_{u},\quad{\mathcal{L}}_{u}(\zeta)=u\blacktriangleright_{\mathsf{ad}_{\mathsf{R}}}\zeta=u\zeta-(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright\zeta)\,(\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright u),\quad\zeta\in\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g}).

Indeed, the map
{\mathcal{L}}_{u}
is a braided derivation: from (
3.19
) the map
\blacktriangleright_{\mathsf{ad}_{\mathsf{R}}}
is an action, then

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{u}(\xi\zeta)

\displaystyle=u\blacktriangleright_{\mathsf{ad}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\xi\zeta)

\displaystyle=({u}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\blacktriangleright_{\mathsf{ad}_{\mathsf{R}}}(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright\xi))\,((\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright{u}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}})\blacktriangleright_{\mathsf{ad}_{\mathsf{R}}}\zeta)

\displaystyle=(u\blacktriangleright_{\mathsf{ad}_{\mathsf{R}}}\xi)\,\zeta+(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright\xi)\,((\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright u)\blacktriangleright_{\mathsf{ad}_{\mathsf{R}}}\zeta)

\displaystyle={\mathcal{L}}_{u}(\xi)\,\zeta+(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright\xi)\,({\mathcal{L}}_{\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright u}\blacktriangleright_{\mathsf{ad}_{\mathsf{R}}}\zeta)

\displaystyle={\mathcal{L}}_{u}(\xi)\,\zeta+(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright\xi)\,(\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright{\mathcal{L}}_{u})(\zeta)

where the last equality uses
K
-equivariance of
{\mathcal{L}}
.
∎

5.4.
Twisting braided Lie algebras

We next consider twist deformations of braided Lie algebras. Let
\mathfrak{g}
be a
braided Lie algebra associated with a triangular Hopf algebra

(K,\mathsf{R})
and
\mathsf{F}
be a twist for
K
. The
K_{\mathsf{F}}
-module
\mathfrak{g}_{F}

(this is just
\mathfrak{g}
with
K
-action; see the construction after
Proposition
4.4
) inherits from
\mathfrak{g}
a twisted bracket:

Proposition 5.14
.

The
K_{\mathsf{F}}
-module
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathsf{F}}
with bilinear map

(5.18)

[\penalty\ ,\penalty\ ]_{\mathsf{F}}=\mathfrak{g}_{\mathsf{F}}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\mathsf{F}}\to\mathfrak{g}_{\mathsf{F}}\penalty\ ,\penalty\ \penalty\ u\otimes v\to[u,v]_{\mathsf{F}}:=[{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright u,{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright v]\penalty\ .

is a
braided Lie algebra associated with
(K_{\mathsf{F}},\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}})
.

Proof.

The
K_{\mathsf{F}}
-equivariance is easily proven using the analogue property for
[\penalty\ ,\penalty\ ]
and recalling from (
4.5
) that
\Delta_{K_{\mathsf{F}}}=\mathsf{F}\Delta_{K}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}
:
for all
u,v\in\mathfrak{g}
,
k\in K_{\mathsf{F}}
,

k\vartriangleright[u,v]_{\mathsf{F}}=[{k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright u,{k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright v]=[{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}{k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{[1]}}\vartriangleright u,{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}{k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{[2]}}\vartriangleright v]=[{k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{[1]}}\vartriangleright u,{k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{[2]}}\vartriangleright v]_{\mathsf{F}}\;.

Similarly, the braided antisymmetry
[u,v]_{\mathsf{F}}=-[{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{\beta}\vartriangleright v,{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\beta}\vartriangleright u]_{\mathsf{F}}
follows from the braided antisymmetry of
[\penalty\ ,\penalty\ ]
and recalling that

\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}=\mathsf{F}_{21}\mathsf{R}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}
.

We are left to prove the Jacobi identity: for all
u,v,w\in\mathfrak{g}_{\mathsf{F}}
, we have

\displaystyle[u,[v,

\displaystyle w]_{\mathsf{F}}]_{\mathsf{F}}=[{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\beta}\vartriangleright u\,,\,[{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\beta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright v\,,\,{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\beta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright w]]

\displaystyle=[[{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\beta}\vartriangleright u\,,\,{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\beta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright v]\,,\,{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\beta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright w]+[\mathsf{R}_{\gamma}{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\beta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright v\,,\,[\mathsf{R}^{\gamma}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\beta}\vartriangleright u\,,\,{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\beta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright w]]

\displaystyle=[{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\beta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright u\,,\,{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\beta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright v]\,,\,{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\beta}\vartriangleright w]]+[\mathsf{R}_{\gamma}{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\beta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright v\,,\,[\mathsf{R}^{\gamma}{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\beta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright u\,,\,{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\beta}\vartriangleright w]]

\displaystyle=[{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\beta}\vartriangleright[{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright u\,,\,{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright v]\,,\,{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\beta}\vartriangleright w]+[{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\beta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\mathsf{R}_{\gamma}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright v\,,\,[{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\beta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\mathsf{R}^{\gamma}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright u\,,\,{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\beta}\vartriangleright w]]

\displaystyle=[[u,v]_{\mathsf{F}},w]_{\mathsf{F}}+[{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\beta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{\gamma}\vartriangleright v\,,\,[{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\beta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\gamma}\vartriangleright u\,,\,{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\beta}\vartriangleright w]]

\displaystyle=[[u,v]_{\mathsf{F}},w]_{\mathsf{F}}+[{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\beta}}{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{\gamma}\vartriangleright v\,,\,[{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\beta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\gamma}\vartriangleright u\,,\,{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\beta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright w]]

\displaystyle=[[u,v]_{\mathsf{F}},w]_{\mathsf{F}}+[{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\beta}}{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{\gamma}\vartriangleright v\,,\,{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\beta}}\vartriangleright[{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\gamma}\vartriangleright u\,,\,{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright w]]

\displaystyle=[[u,v]_{\mathsf{F}},w]_{\mathsf{F}}+[{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{\gamma}\vartriangleright v,[{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\gamma}\vartriangleright u,w]_{\mathsf{F}}]_{\mathsf{F}}.

For the first two equalities we used the
K
-equivariance and Jacobi identity for
[\penalty\ ,\penalty\ ]
, for the third the twist property (
4.4
), for the forth the
K
-equivariance and the quasi-cocommutativity of
K
. Then

\mathsf{R}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}={\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{21}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}
and the twist property end the proof.
∎

Example 5.15
.

When
\mathfrak{g}
is a
K
-module algebra
A
as in Lemma
5.2
,
its braided commutator is twisted to

\displaystyle[a,b]_{\mathsf{F}}

\displaystyle=({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright_{A}a)({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright_{A}b)-(\mathsf{R}_{\beta}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright_{A}b)\,(\mathsf{R}^{\beta}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright_{A}a)

\displaystyle=({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright_{A}a)({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright_{A}b)-({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{\beta}\vartriangleright_{A}b)\,({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\beta}\vartriangleright_{A}a)

\displaystyle=a{\,\raisebox{1.5pt}{\scalebox{0.55}[0.55]{\mbox{$\bullet_{\mbox{$\mathsf{F}\,$}}$}}}}b-({\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{\beta}\vartriangleright_{A}b){\,\raisebox{1.5pt}{\scalebox{0.55}[0.55]{\mbox{$\bullet_{\mbox{$\mathsf{F}\,$}}$}}}}({\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\beta}\vartriangleright_{A}a).

Since
\vartriangleright_{A}
and
\vartriangleright_{A_{\mathsf{F}}}
coincide, this shows that the braided Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathsf{F}}
is just the twisted
K_{\mathsf{F}}
-module algebra
A_{\mathsf{F}}
with braided commutator defined as in (
5.1
) but using the universal
R
-matrix
{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}
of
K_{\mathsf{F}}
and the product in
A_{\mathsf{F}}
.

\blacksquare

Example 5.16
.

As a particular case of the previous example, consider the
K
-module
algebra
({\rm{Hom}}(V,V),\circ)
of linear maps of a
K
-module
V
with
action (
2.1
). The twist deformation of the braided Lie algebra

(({\rm{Hom}}(V,V),\circ),[\,,\,])
is the braided Lie algebra

(({\rm{Hom}}_{\mathsf{F}}(V,V),\circ_{\mathsf{F}}),[\,,\,]_{\mathsf{F}})
,
that is the
K_{\mathsf{F}}
-module algebra
({\rm{Hom}}_{\mathsf{F}}(V,V),\circ_{\mathsf{F}})
,
see (
4.13
), with braided commutator

[\psi,\lambda]_{\mathsf{F}}=\psi\circ_{\mathsf{F}}\lambda-({\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright_{{\rm{Hom}}(V,V)}\lambda)\circ_{\mathsf{F}}({\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright_{{\rm{Hom}}(V,V)}\psi)\;.

We prove that it is isomorphic to the braided Lie algebra
(({\rm{Hom}}(V_{\mathsf{F}},V_{\mathsf{F}}),\circ),[\,,\,]_{{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}})
associated with the triangular Hopf algebra
(K_{\mathsf{F}},\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}})
.
Indeed, the
K_{\mathsf{F}}
-module algebra isomorphism
\mathcal{D}:({\rm{Hom}}_{\mathsf{F}}(V,V),\circ_{\mathsf{F}})\to({\rm{Hom}}(V_{\mathsf{F}},V_{\mathsf{F}}),\circ)
in Proposition
4.6
is also a braided Lie algebra isomorphism:

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}\big([\psi,\lambda]_{\mathsf{F}}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{D}(\psi)\circ\mathcal{D}(\lambda)-\mathcal{D}({\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright_{{\rm{Hom}}(V,V)}\lambda)\circ\mathcal{D}({\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright_{{\rm{Hom}}(V,V)}\psi)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{D}(\psi)\circ\mathcal{D}(\lambda)-{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright_{{\rm{Hom}}(V_{\mathsf{F}},V_{\mathsf{F}})}\mathcal{D}(\lambda)\circ{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright_{{\rm{Hom}}(V_{\mathsf{F}},V_{\mathsf{F}})}\mathcal{D}(\psi)

\displaystyle=[\mathcal{D}(\psi),\mathcal{D}(\lambda)]_{{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}}.

Here we used that
\mathcal{D}
is an algebra map and a morphism of

K_{\mathsf{F}}
-modules.

Furthermore, from the
K
-braided Poisson algebra structure
({\rm{Hom}}(V,V),\circ,[\,,\,])
, we have that

({\rm{Hom}}_{\mathsf{F}}(V,V),\circ_{\mathsf{F}},[\,,\,]_{\mathsf{F}})
, is a
K_{\mathsf{F}}
-braided Poisson
algebra. Indeed, the derivation property of the bracket
[\,,\,]_{\mathsf{F}}

with respect to the multiplication
\circ_{\mathsf{F}}
is proven
exactly as in the proof of the Jacobi identity of Proposition

5.14
(just replace the
second bracket
[\penalty\ ,\penalty\ ]_{\mathsf{F}}
with the multiplication
\circ_{\mathsf{F}}
).
Then it follows that
\mathcal{D}:({\rm{Hom}}_{\mathsf{F}}(V,V),\circ_{\mathsf{F}},[\penalty\ ,\penalty\ ]_{\mathsf{F}})\to({\rm{Hom}}(V_{\mathsf{F}},V_{\mathsf{F}}),\circ,[\penalty\ ,\penalty\ ]_{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}})
is a
K_{\mathsf{F}}
-braided Poisson
algebra isomorphism

\blacksquare

The braided Hopf algebra
\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g})_{\mathsf{F}}
associated with

(K_{\mathsf{F}},\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}})
is given, as in Proposition
4.11
, by the twist deformation
of the universal enveloping algebra
\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g})
of the braided Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
.
We now show that it coincides (up to isomorphism) with the universal enveloping algebra

\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathsf{F}})
of the braided Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathsf{F}}
, given as in Proposition
5.14
. In other words,
the universal enveloping algebra construction commutes with twist
deformation:
\mathfrak{g}\to\mathfrak{g}_{\mathsf{F}}\to\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathsf{F}})
equals
\mathfrak{g}\to\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g})\to\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g})_{\mathsf{F}}
.

The isomorphism

\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathsf{F}})\simeq\mathcal{U}(g)_{\mathsf{F}}

is induced from the
K_{\mathsf{F}}
-module isomorphism

\varphi^{(2)}:=\varphi_{\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}}:\mathfrak{g}_{\mathsf{F}}\otimes_{\mathsf{F}}\mathfrak{g}_{\mathsf{F}}\to(\mathfrak{g}\otimes\mathfrak{g})_{\mathsf{F}}\penalty\ ,\quad u\otimes_{\mathsf{F}}v\mapsto{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright_{\mathfrak{g}}u\otimes{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright_{\mathfrak{g}}v\penalty\ ,

given as in (
4.7
), and its lifts
\varphi^{(n)}
to
n
-th tensor products

\varphi^{(n)}:\mathfrak{g}_{\mathsf{F}}^{\otimes_{\mathsf{F}}n}\to(\mathfrak{g}^{\otimes n})_{\mathsf{F}}
, for
n\in\mathbb{N}
, defined recursively,
for
n\geq 2
, by

\varphi^{(n+1)}=\varphi_{\mathfrak{g},(\mathfrak{g}^{\otimes n})}\circ(\mathrm{id}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathsf{F}}}\otimes_{\mathsf{F}}\varphi^{(n)}),

with the maps
\varphi_{\mathfrak{g},(\mathfrak{g}^{\otimes n})}
again as in (
4.7
), and
\varphi^{(0)}:=\mathrm{id}_{\mathds{k}}
,

\varphi^{(1)}:=\mathrm{id}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathsf{F}}}
.
Explicitly,

\varphi^{(n)}(u_{1}\otimes_{\mathsf{F}}\ldots u_{n-1}\otimes_{\mathsf{F}}u_{n})=\\
={\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha_{1}}\vartriangleright u_{1}\otimes{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha_{1}}\vartriangleright(\ldots{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha_{n-2}}\vartriangleright u_{n-2}\otimes{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha_{n-2}}\vartriangleright({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha_{n-1}}\vartriangleright u_{n-1}\otimes{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha_{n-1}}\vartriangleright u_{n}))\,,

for all
u_{i}\in\mathfrak{g}_{\mathsf{F}}
,
i=1,\ldots n
.
Property (
4.8
) of the isomorphisms
\varphi_{-,-}
, generalized to
n
-th tensor products, gives

Lemma 5.17
.

For integers
p,q\in\mathbb{N}
with
p+q=n
,

(5.19)

\varphi^{(n)}=\varphi_{\mathfrak{g}^{\otimes p},\mathfrak{g}^{\otimes q}}\circ(\varphi^{(p)}\otimes_{\mathsf{F}}\varphi^{(q)})\,.

Proof.

By induction: the identity holds for
n\leq 2
;
assume it holds for
n
, then

\displaystyle\varphi^{(n+1)}

\displaystyle=\varphi_{\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}^{\otimes n}}\circ(\mathrm{id}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathsf{F}}}\otimes_{\mathsf{F}}\varphi^{(n)})

\displaystyle=\varphi_{\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}^{\otimes n}}\circ(\mathrm{id}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathsf{F}}}\otimes_{\mathsf{F}}\varphi_{\mathfrak{g}^{\otimes p},\mathfrak{g}^{\otimes q}}\circ(\varphi^{(p)}\otimes_{\mathsf{F}}\varphi^{(q)}))

\displaystyle=\varphi_{\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}^{\otimes n}}\circ(\mathrm{id}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathsf{F}}}\otimes_{\mathsf{F}}\varphi_{\mathfrak{g}^{\otimes p},\mathfrak{g}^{\otimes q}})\circ(\mathrm{id}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathsf{F}}}\otimes_{\mathsf{F}}\varphi^{(p)}\otimes_{\mathsf{F}}\varphi^{(q)})

\displaystyle=\varphi_{\mathfrak{g}^{\otimes p+1},\mathfrak{g}^{\otimes q}}\circ(\varphi_{\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}^{\otimes p}}\otimes_{\mathsf{F}}\mathrm{id}_{(\mathfrak{g}^{\otimes q})_{\mathsf{F}}})\circ(\mathrm{id}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathsf{F}}}\otimes_{\mathsf{F}}\varphi^{(p)}\otimes_{\mathsf{F}}\varphi^{(q)})

\displaystyle=\varphi_{\mathfrak{g}^{\otimes p+1},\mathfrak{g}^{\otimes q}}\circ(\varphi^{(p+1)}\otimes_{\mathsf{F}}\varphi^{(q)})\penalty\

where for the fourth equality we have used property (
4.8
) of the isomorphisms
\varphi_{-,-}
.
∎

Proposition 5.18
.

The braided Hopf algebras
\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g})_{\mathsf{F}}
and
\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathsf{F}})
associated with the triangular Hopf algebra

(K_{\mathsf{F}},\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}})
are isomorphic.

Proof.

The isomorphisms

\varphi^{(n)}:\mathfrak{g}_{\mathsf{F}}^{\otimes_{\mathsf{F}}n}{\longrightarrow}(\mathfrak{g}^{\otimes n})_{\mathsf{F}}
induce the
isomorphism

{\varphi^{\bullet}}:=\oplus_{n\geq 0}\varphi^{(n)}:T(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathsf{F}})\to T(\mathfrak{g})_{\mathsf{F}}

of (graded)
K_{\mathsf{F}}
-modules. We show it is a braided Hopf algebra isomorphism.

Let us denote by
m_{\otimes_{\mathsf{F}}}
the multiplication in the tensor algebra
T(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathsf{F}})
,
and by
(m_{\otimes})_{\mathsf{F}}
the one in
T(\mathfrak{g})_{\mathsf{F}}
given by

(m_{\otimes})_{\mathsf{F}}(\tilde{\zeta},\tilde{\xi}):={\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright\tilde{\zeta}\otimes{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright\tilde{\xi}

on
homogeneous elements

\tilde{\zeta}\in(\mathfrak{g}^{\otimes n})_{\mathsf{F}}
,
\tilde{\xi}\in(\mathfrak{g}^{\otimes m})_{\mathsf{F}}
, as in (
4
).
Then for all

\zeta\in\mathfrak{g}_{\mathsf{F}}^{\otimes_{\mathsf{F}}n},\xi\in\mathfrak{g}_{\mathsf{F}}^{\otimes_{\mathsf{F}}m}
,

\displaystyle(m_{\otimes})_{\mathsf{F}}\circ(\varphi^{(n)}\times\varphi^{(m)})(\zeta,\xi)

\displaystyle=(m_{\otimes})_{\mathsf{F}}(\varphi^{(n)}(\zeta),\varphi^{(m)}(\xi))

\displaystyle={\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright\varphi^{(n)}(\zeta)\otimes{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright\varphi^{(m)}(\xi)

\displaystyle=\varphi_{\mathfrak{g}^{\otimes n},\mathfrak{g}^{\otimes m}}\circ(\varphi^{(n)}\otimes_{\mathsf{F}}\varphi^{(m)})(\zeta\otimes_{\mathsf{F}}\xi)

\displaystyle=\varphi^{(n+m)}\circ m_{\otimes_{\mathsf{F}}}(\zeta,\xi)

using (
5.19
) for the last equality.
This implies
(m_{\otimes})_{\mathsf{F}}\circ(\varphi^{\bullet}\times\varphi^{\bullet})=\varphi^{\bullet}\circ m_{\otimes_{\mathsf{F}}}
so that
\varphi^{\bullet}:T(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathsf{F}})\to T(\mathfrak{g})_{\mathsf{F}}
is
an algebra isomorphism, in fact a
K_{\mathsf{F}}
-module algebra isomorphism.

We are left to show that
\varphi^{\bullet}
is a coalgebra morphism. Since the counits are zero (but on
\mathds{k}
), the condition

\varepsilon_{T(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathsf{F}})}=\varepsilon_{T(\mathfrak{g})_{\mathsf{F}}}\circ\varphi^{\bullet}
is trivially true.
To show the compatibility with the coproducts,

(\varphi^{\bullet}\boxtimes_{\mathsf{F}}\varphi^{\bullet})\circ\Delta_{T(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathsf{F}})}=\Delta_{T(\mathfrak{g})_{\mathsf{F}}}\circ\varphi^{\bullet}
,
it suffices to check it on degree zero and one elements since both sides are braided algebra maps.
Being
\varphi^{(0)}
and
\varphi^{(1)}
both identity maps, this reduces to show that
\Delta_{T(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathsf{F}})}=\Delta_{T(\mathfrak{g})_{\mathsf{F}}}
on
u\in\mathfrak{g}_{\mathsf{F}}
. But this is immediate. Indeed,

T(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathsf{F}})
has coproduct
\Delta_{T(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathsf{F}})}
given by

\Delta_{T(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathsf{F}})}(u)=u\boxtimes_{\mathsf{F}}1+1\boxtimes_{\mathsf{F}}u
for
u\in\mathfrak{g}_{\mathsf{F}}
, (and extended as a braided
algebra map, see Proposition
5.4
).
On the other hand, the coproduct on
T(\mathfrak{g})_{\mathsf{F}}
is
the twist deformation as (
4.12
) of the coproduct of
T(\mathfrak{g})
,

\Delta_{T(\mathfrak{g})_{\mathsf{F}}}:=\varphi_{T(\mathfrak{g}),T(\mathfrak{g})}^{-1}\circ\Gamma(\Delta_{T(\mathfrak{g})}):\quad\tilde{\xi}\longmapsto{\tilde{\xi}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{[1]}}\boxtimes_{\mathsf{F}}{\tilde{\xi}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{[2]}}:=(\mathsf{F}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright{\tilde{\xi}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}})\boxtimes_{\mathsf{F}}(\mathsf{F}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright{\tilde{\xi}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}),

which reduces to
\Delta_{T(\mathfrak{g})_{\mathsf{F}}}(u)=u\boxtimes_{\mathsf{F}}1+1\boxtimes_{\mathsf{F}}u
in degree one.

The braided Hopf algebra isomorphism
\varphi^{\bullet}
induces such an isomorphism

\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathsf{F}})\simeq\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g})_{\mathsf{F}}
on the quotients.
Firstly, the ideal
T(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathsf{F}})\supseteq I_{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathsf{F}}}=\langle u\otimes_{\mathsf{F}}v-{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{\!\beta}\vartriangleright v\otimes_{\mathsf{F}}{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\beta}\vartriangleright u-[u,v]_{\mathsf{F}}\rangle

is mapped by
\varphi^{\bullet}
to the ideal

T(\mathfrak{g})_{\mathsf{F}}\supseteq(I_{\mathfrak{g}})_{\mathsf{F}}=\langle u\otimes v-\mathsf{R}_{\beta}\vartriangleright v\otimes\mathsf{R}^{\beta}\vartriangleright u-[u,v]\rangle
.
For all
u,v\in\mathfrak{g}_{\mathsf{F}}
, since

\varphi^{(1)}=\mathrm{id}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathsf{F}}}
and using
{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{21}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}=\mathsf{R}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}
,
we compute

\displaystyle\varphi^{\bullet}(u\otimes_{\mathsf{F}}v-{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{\beta}

\displaystyle\vartriangleright v\otimes_{\mathsf{F}}{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\beta}\vartriangleright u-[u,v]_{\mathsf{F}})

\displaystyle={\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright u\otimes{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright v-{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{\beta}\vartriangleright v\otimes{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\beta}\vartriangleright u-[u,v]_{\mathsf{F}}

\displaystyle={\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright u\otimes{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright v-\mathsf{R}_{\beta}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright v\otimes\mathsf{R}^{\beta}{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright u-[{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright u,{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright v].

Since
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathsf{F}}
is a
K_{\mathsf{F}}
-module, the last line is a sum over the index
\alpha
of generators
of
(I_{\mathfrak{g}})_{\mathsf{F}}
.

The algebra map property of

\varphi^{\bullet}
then implies that the ideal
I_{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathsf{F}}}\subseteq T(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathsf{F}})

is isomorphic to the ideal
(I_{\mathfrak{g}})_{\mathsf{F}}\subseteq T(\mathfrak{g})_{\mathsf{F}}
. Both

I_{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathsf{F}}}
and
(I_{\mathfrak{g}})_{\mathsf{F}}
are
K_{\mathsf{F}}
-modules, hence
\varphi^{\bullet}

induces a
K_{\mathsf{F}}
-equivariant algebra isomorphism on the quotients

\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathsf{F}})=T(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathsf{F}})/I_{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathsf{F}}}\simeq T(\mathfrak{g})_{\mathsf{F}}/(I_{\mathfrak{g}})_{\mathsf{F}}\penalty\ .

This is actually a braided Hopf algebra isomorphism
since both
I_{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathsf{F}}}
and
(I_{\mathfrak{g}})_{\mathsf{F}}
are also coideals.
The proposition is proven by observing that as braided Hopf algebras,

T(\mathfrak{g})_{\mathsf{F}}/(I_{\mathfrak{g}})_{\mathsf{F}}\simeq\Big(T(\mathfrak{g})/I_{\mathfrak{g}}\Big)_{\mathsf{F}}=\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g})_{\mathsf{F}}

where
T(\mathfrak{g})_{\mathsf{F}}/(I_{\mathfrak{g}})_{\mathsf{F}}\simeq\Big(T(\mathfrak{g})/I_{\mathfrak{g}}\Big)_{\mathsf{F}}
via the identity map of
\mathds{k}
-modules.
∎

5.5.
Twisting braided derivations

Let
A
be a
K
-module algebra and
A_{\mathsf{F}}
the corresponding

K_{\mathsf{F}}
-module algebra obtained by twisting the product as in
(
4
).
As in Example
5.16
there is an
isomorphism of braided Lie algebras:

Theorem 5.19
.

The braided Lie algebras
(\mathrm{Der}{(A)}_{\mathsf{F}},[\,,\,]_{\mathsf{F}})
and

(\mathrm{Der}{(A_{\mathsf{F}})},[\,,\,]_{{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}})
are isomorphic via the map

\mathcal{D}:\mathrm{Der}{(A)}_{\mathsf{F}}\to\mathrm{Der}{(A_{\mathsf{F}})}
, the restriction of
the isomorphism
\mathcal{D}:(({\rm{Hom}}(A,A)_{\mathsf{F}},\circ_{\mathsf{F}}),[\,,\,]_{\mathsf{F}})\to(({\rm{Hom}}(A_{\mathsf{F}},A_{\mathsf{F}}),\circ),[\,,\,]_{{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}})
.

Proof.

Since
\mathrm{Der}{(A)}_{\mathsf{F}}\subset{\rm{Hom}}(A,A)_{\mathsf{F}}
and
\mathrm{Der}{(A_{\mathsf{F}})}\subset{\rm{Hom}}(A_{\mathsf{F}},A_{\mathsf{F}})
are braided Lie subalgebras,
we just need to prove that

\mathcal{D}:\mathrm{Der}{(A)}_{\mathsf{F}}\to\mathrm{Der}{(A_{\mathsf{F}})}
is a bijection between the sets

\mathrm{Der}{(A)}=\mathrm{Der}{(A)}_{\mathsf{F}}
of
(K,\mathsf{R})
-braided derivations of
A
and

\mathrm{Der}{(A_{\mathsf{F}})}
of
(K_{\mathsf{F}},\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}})
-braided derivations of
A_{\mathsf{F}}
.
Recall that a linear map
\psi\in{\rm{Hom}}(A,A)
is a
(K,\mathsf{R})
-braided derivation if and only if (
5.6
) holds:

(5.20)

[\psi,\ell_{a}]_{\mathsf{R}}=\ell_{\psi(a)}

for all
a\in A
. This condition is
linear in
\psi
and
a
and, since
A
and
\mathrm{Der}{(A)}
are

K
-modules, it implies

(5.21)

[{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright\psi,\ell_{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright a}]_{\mathsf{R}}=\ell_{({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright\psi)({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright a)}

for all
a\in A
. Similarly, applying
\mathsf{F}
to
\mathsf{F}^{-1}\vartriangleright(\psi\otimes a)
we see
that (
5.20
) is equivalent to (
5.21
).
In turn, from the
K
-equivariance of
\ell
the latter is equivalent to

[\psi,\ell_{a}]_{\mathsf{F}}:=[{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright\psi,{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright\ell_{a}]_{\mathsf{R}}=\ell_{\mathcal{D}(\psi)(a)}\penalty\ .

Applying the isomorphism
\mathcal{D}:(({\rm{Hom}}(A,A)_{\mathsf{F}},\circ_{\mathsf{F}}),[\,,\,]_{\mathsf{F}})\to(({\rm{Hom}}(A_{\mathsf{F}},A_{\mathsf{F}}),\circ),[\,,\,]_{{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}})
of
K_{\mathsf{F}}
-braided Lie algebras
shows
that (
5.20
) is equivalent to

(5.22)

[\mathcal{D}(\psi),\mathcal{D}(\ell_{a})]_{{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}}=\mathcal{D}(\ell_{\mathcal{D}(\psi)(a)})\penalty\ .

Thus
\psi
is a
(K,\mathsf{R})
-braided derivation if and only if (
5.22
) holds. The theorem is proven by showing that the latter is equivalent to
D(\psi)
being a
(K_{\mathsf{F}},\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}})
-braided derivation.
To this end we
evaluate (
5.22
) on any
a^{\prime}\in A
and, using

K
-equivariance of
\ell
, we obtain

\mathcal{D}(\psi)\big(\mathcal{D}(\ell_{a})(a^{\prime})\big)-({\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright\mathcal{D}(\ell_{a}))\big(({\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright\mathcal{D}(\psi))(a^{\prime})\big)\,=\,\mathcal{D}(\psi)(a){\,\raisebox{1.5pt}{\scalebox{0.55}[0.55]{\mbox{$\bullet_{\mbox{$\mathsf{F}\,$}}$}}}}a^{\prime}

that using also
K_{\mathsf{F}}
-equivariance of
\mathcal{D}
is equivalent to

(5.23)

\mathcal{D}(\psi)(a{\,\raisebox{1.5pt}{\scalebox{0.55}[0.55]{\mbox{$\bullet_{\mbox{$\mathsf{F}\,$}}$}}}}a^{\prime})=\mathcal{D}(\psi)(a){\,\raisebox{1.5pt}{\scalebox{0.55}[0.55]{\mbox{$\bullet_{\mbox{$\mathsf{F}\,$}}$}}}}a^{\prime}+({\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright a){\,\raisebox{1.5pt}{\scalebox{0.55}[0.55]{\mbox{$\bullet_{\mbox{$\mathsf{F}\,$}}$}}}}({\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright\mathcal{D}(\psi))(a^{\prime})\penalty\ .

The implication (
5.20
)
\Rightarrow
(
5.23
) shows that
the bijection
\mathcal{D}:{\rm{Hom}}(A,A)_{\mathsf{F}}\to{\rm{Hom}}(A_{\mathsf{F}},A_{\mathsf{F}})
restricts
to the injection
\mathcal{D}:\mathrm{Der}{(A)}_{\mathsf{F}}\to\mathrm{Der}{(A_{\mathsf{F}})}
(recall that

{\rm{Hom}}(A,A)={\rm{Hom}}_{\mathsf{F}}(A,A)
as sets). Since any
element of
{\rm{Hom}}(A_{\mathsf{F}},A_{\mathsf{F}})
can be written as
\mathcal{D}(\psi)
for some

\psi\in{\rm{Hom}}(A,A)_{\mathsf{F}}
, the
implication (
5.23
)
\Rightarrow
(
5.20
) shows that

\mathcal{D}:\mathrm{Der}{(A)}_{\mathsf{F}}\to\mathrm{Der}{(A_{\mathsf{F}})}
is a surjection.
∎

When
A
is quasi-commutative, the
K
-braided Lie
algebra
\mathrm{Der}{(A)}
has an
A
-module structure defined in (
5.9
)
that is compatible with the Lie bracket of
\mathrm{Der}{(A)}
(cf. Proposition
5.8
).
This implies that also the
K_{\mathsf{F}}
-braided Lie
algebras
\mathrm{Der}{(A)}_{\mathsf{F}}
and
\mathrm{Der}{(A_{\mathsf{F}})}
have compatible
A_{\mathsf{F}}
-module
structures and the isomorphism
\mathcal{D}:\mathrm{Der}{(A)}_{\mathsf{F}}\to\mathrm{Der}{(A_{\mathsf{F}})}
maps one
into the other.
The
(K,A)
-relative Hopf module
\mathrm{Der}{(A)}
with
A
-module structure (
5.9
) is twisted to the

(K_{\mathsf{F}},A_{\mathsf{F}})
-relative Hopf module
\mathrm{Der}{(A)}_{\mathsf{F}}
with

(5.24)

a\cdot_{\mathsf{F}}\psi:=({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright_{A}a)\,({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright_{\mathrm{Der}{(A)}}\psi)

for all
a\in A_{\mathsf{F}}
,

\psi\in\mathrm{Der}{(A)}_{\mathsf{F}}
, that is
a\cdot_{\mathsf{F}}\psi=\ell_{a}\circ_{\mathsf{F}}\psi
. The braided derivation property of the bracket
with respect to this module structure reads, for all
\psi,\psi^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Der}{(A)}_{\mathsf{F}}
,
a\in A_{\mathsf{F}}
,

(5.25)

[\psi,a\cdot_{\mathsf{F}}\psi^{\prime}]_{\mathsf{F}}=[\psi,a]_{\mathsf{F}}\cdot_{\mathsf{F}}\psi^{\prime}+({\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright a)\cdot_{\mathsf{F}}[{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright\psi,\psi^{\prime}]_{\mathsf{F}}

where
[\psi,a]_{\mathsf{F}}:=({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright\psi)({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright a)
in agreement with
[\psi,\ell_{a}]_{\mathsf{F}}=[{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright\psi,{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright\ell_{a}]=\ell_{({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright\psi)({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright a)}
. We recall that

({\rm{Hom}}(A,A)_{\mathsf{F}},\circ_{\mathsf{F}},[\,,\,]_{\mathsf{F}})
, is a
K_{\mathsf{F}}
-braided Poisson
algebra (cf. Example
5.16
). Then the proof of (
5.25
)
is along the proof of
Proposition
5.8
where now we consider a

K_{\mathsf{F}}
-braided Poisson algebra
(P_{\mathsf{F}},\cdot_{\mathsf{F}},[\penalty\ ,\penalty\ ]_{\mathsf{F}})
and
the
K
-equivariant map
\ell:A\to{\rm{Hom}}(A,A)
, with
\ell_{a}
defined in
(
5.5
), is seen as a

K_{\mathsf{F}}
-equivariant map

\ell:A_{\mathsf{F}}\to{\rm{Hom}}(A,A)_{\mathsf{F}}
. Setting

p=\mathrm{id}_{A_{\mathsf{F}}}
,
q=\psi
,
p^{\prime}=\ell_{a}
,
q^{\prime}=\psi^{\prime}
in the

K_{\mathsf{F}}
-braided Poisson algebra version of (
5.11
) we
obtain

\begin{split}[\psi,a\cdot_{\mathsf{F}}\psi^{\prime}]_{\mathsf{F}}=[\psi,\ell_{a}\circ_{\mathsf{F}}\psi^{\prime}]_{\mathsf{F}}&=[\psi,\ell_{a}]_{\mathsf{F}}\circ_{\mathsf{F}}\psi^{\prime}+({\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright\ell_{a})\circ_{\mathsf{F}}[{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright\psi,\psi^{\prime}]_{\mathsf{F}}\\
&=[{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright\psi,\ell_{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright a}]\circ_{\mathsf{F}}\psi^{\prime}+\ell_{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\beta}{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright a}\circ{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\beta}\vartriangleright[{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright\psi,\psi^{\prime}]_{\mathsf{F}}\\
&=\ell_{({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright\psi)({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright a)}\circ_{\mathsf{F}}\psi^{\prime}+({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\beta}{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright a)\>\mathsf{F}_{\beta}\vartriangleright[{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright\psi,\psi^{\prime}]_{\mathsf{F}}\\
&=[\psi,a]_{\mathsf{F}}\cdot_{\mathsf{F}}\psi^{\prime}+({\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright a)\cdot_{\mathsf{F}}[{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright\psi,\psi^{\prime}]_{\mathsf{F}}\penalty\ .\end{split}

This establishes the braided derivation property (
5.25
). More generally, setting

p=\ell_{a}
,
q=\psi
,
p^{\prime}=\ell_{a^{\prime}}
,
q^{\prime}=\psi^{\prime}
in (
5.11
) we obtain

(5.26)

\begin{split}[a\cdot_{\mathsf{F}}\psi,a^{\prime}\cdot_{\mathsf{F}}\psi^{\prime}]_{\mathsf{F}}=&\penalty\ a\cdot_{\mathsf{F}}[\psi,a^{\prime}]_{\mathsf{F}}\cdot_{\mathsf{F}}\psi^{\prime}+a\cdot_{\mathsf{F}}({\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright a^{\prime})\cdot_{\mathsf{F}}[{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright\psi,\psi^{\prime}]_{\mathsf{F}}\\
&+{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{\beta}\>\!{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright a^{\prime}\cdot_{\mathsf{F}}[{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{\delta}{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{\gamma}\vartriangleright\psi^{\prime}\,,\,\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}{}^{\delta}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}{}^{\beta}\vartriangleright a]_{\mathsf{F}}\cdot_{\mathsf{F}}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}{}^{\gamma}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}{}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright\psi\penalty\ .\end{split}

The above shows that we have a
(K_{\mathsf{F}},\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}})
-braided Lie algebra
\mathrm{Der}{(A)}_{\mathsf{F}}
with compatible
left
A_{\mathsf{F}}
-module structure (
5.24
). In addition
we have also that
\mathrm{Der}{(A_{\mathsf{F}})}
is a

(K_{\mathsf{F}},\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}})
-braided Lie algebra with compatible
A_{F}
-module
structure defined by

(5.27)

(a{\,\raisebox{1.5pt}{\scalebox{0.55}[0.55]{\mbox{$\bullet_{\mbox{$\mathsf{F}\,$}}$}}}}\tilde{\psi})(a^{\prime}):=a{\,\raisebox{1.5pt}{\scalebox{0.55}[0.55]{\mbox{$\bullet_{\mbox{$\mathsf{F}\,$}}$}}}}\tilde{\psi}(a^{\prime})

for all
a,a^{\prime}\in A_{\mathsf{F}},\tilde{\psi}\in\mathrm{Der}{(A_{\mathsf{F}})}
, as in (
5.9
).
Indeed
if
A
is
K
-quasi-commutative then
A_{\mathsf{F}}
is

K_{\mathsf{F}}
-quasi-commutative so that we can apply Proposition
5.8
to this
case.

Corollary 5.20
.

If the
K
-module algebra
A
is quasi-commutative the braided Lie
algebra isomorphism
\mathcal{D}:(\mathrm{Der}{(A)}_{\mathsf{F}},[\,,\,]_{\mathsf{F}})\rightarrow(\mathrm{Der}{(A_{\mathsf{F}})},[\,,\,]_{{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}})
of Theorem
5.19
is also an isomorphism of
the
A_{\mathsf{F}}
-modules
\mathrm{Der}{(A)}_{\mathsf{F}}
and
\mathrm{Der}{(A_{\mathsf{F}})}
.

Proof.

For any
a\in A_{\mathsf{F}}
we have
\ell_{a}:A\to A
and
\mathcal{D}(\ell_{a}):A_{\mathsf{F}}\to A_{\mathsf{F}}
. Let
\mathcal{D}(\psi)
be an element of
\mathrm{Der}{(A_{\mathsf{F}})}
, we
show that
a{\,\raisebox{1.5pt}{\scalebox{0.55}[0.55]{\mbox{$\bullet_{\mbox{$\mathsf{F}\,$}}$}}}}\mathcal{D}(\psi)=\mathcal{D}(\ell_{a})\circ\mathcal{D}(\psi)
. For any

a^{\prime}\in A_{\mathsf{F}}
,

\begin{split}\mathcal{D}(\ell_{a})\circ\mathcal{D}(\psi)\>\!(a^{\prime})&=\mathcal{D}(\ell_{a})\big(\mathcal{D}(\psi)(a^{\prime})\big)=\ell_{{\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright a}\big({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright\big(\mathcal{D}(\psi)(a^{\prime})\big)\big)=a{\,\raisebox{1.5pt}{\scalebox{0.55}[0.55]{\mbox{$\bullet_{\mbox{$\mathsf{F}\,$}}$}}}}\big(\mathcal{D}(\psi)(a^{\prime})\big)\\
&=(a{\,\raisebox{1.5pt}{\scalebox{0.55}[0.55]{\mbox{$\bullet_{\mbox{$\mathsf{F}\,$}}$}}}}\mathcal{D}(\psi))(a^{\prime})\penalty\ .\end{split}

We then have for any
\psi\in\mathrm{Der}{(A)}
,

\mathcal{D}(a\cdot_{\mathsf{F}}\psi)=\mathcal{D}(\ell_{a}\circ_{\mathsf{F}}\psi)=\mathcal{D}(\ell_{a})\circ\mathcal{D}(\psi)=a{\,\raisebox{1.5pt}{\scalebox{0.55}[0.55]{\mbox{$\bullet_{\mbox{$\mathsf{F}\,$}}$}}}}\mathcal{D}(\psi)
.
∎

We denote
\tilde{\psi}=\mathcal{D}(\psi)
for the image of
\psi\in\mathrm{Der}{(A)}_{\mathsf{F}}
.
When applying
\mathcal{D}
to (
5.26
), we obtain the
equality,

(5.28)

\displaystyle[a{\,\raisebox{1.5pt}{\scalebox{0.55}[0.55]{\mbox{$\bullet_{\mbox{$\mathsf{F}\,$}}$}}}}\tilde{\psi},a^{\prime}{\,\raisebox{1.5pt}{\scalebox{0.55}[0.55]{\mbox{$\bullet_{\mbox{$\mathsf{F}\,$}}$}}}}\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}]_{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}=

\displaystyle\penalty\ a{\,\raisebox{1.5pt}{\scalebox{0.55}[0.55]{\mbox{$\bullet_{\mbox{$\mathsf{F}\,$}}$}}}}\tilde{\psi}(a^{\prime}){\,\raisebox{1.5pt}{\scalebox{0.55}[0.55]{\mbox{$\bullet_{\mbox{$\mathsf{F}\,$}}$}}}}\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}+a{\,\raisebox{1.5pt}{\scalebox{0.55}[0.55]{\mbox{$\bullet_{\mbox{$\mathsf{F}\,$}}$}}}}({\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright a^{\prime}){\,\raisebox{1.5pt}{\scalebox{0.55}[0.55]{\mbox{$\bullet_{\mbox{$\mathsf{F}\,$}}$}}}}[{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright\tilde{\psi},\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}]_{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}

\displaystyle+{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{\beta}{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright a^{\prime}{\,\raisebox{1.5pt}{\scalebox{0.55}[0.55]{\mbox{$\bullet_{\mbox{$\mathsf{F}\,$}}$}}}}[{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{\delta}{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{\gamma}\vartriangleright\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}\,,\,{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\delta}{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\beta}\vartriangleright a]_{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}{\,\raisebox{1.5pt}{\scalebox{0.55}[0.55]{\mbox{$\bullet_{\mbox{$\mathsf{F}\,$}}$}}}}{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\gamma}{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright\tilde{\psi}

for all
\tilde{\psi}
,
\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Der}{(A_{\mathsf{F}})}
.
We used that

[\psi,a]_{\mathsf{F}}={({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright\psi)({\overline{\mathsf{F}}}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright a)}=\mathcal{D}(\psi)(a)
.

6.
Gauge group of Hopf–Galois extensions

The notion of gauge group of a quantum principal bundle

A^{coH}\subset A
has been studied in
[
11
,
12
]

as the group of invertible
H
-equivariant unital maps
A\to A
. These are not
required to be algebra maps and in the case of a commutative principal
bundle the resulting gauge group is much bigger than the usual
commutative one (see
[
6
, Ex. 3.1]
).

Here, along the lines of
[
6
]

we follow a different route to the gauge group.
We first study the group of unital
H
-equivariant

algebra
maps (right
H
-comodule algebra maps) from
A
to
A
.
Examples are provided that show when this notion is too
restrictive and when it is not.
In the next section we introduce
K
-equivariant Hopf–Galois extensions
A^{coH}\subset A
,
with
K
an external triangular Hopf algebra of symmetries of the
extension. In that context we provide a more general definition of
gauge symmetries as braided Hopf algebras.

6.1.
Hopf–Galois extensions

We consider noncommutative principal bundles as Hopf–Galois
extensions. These are
H
-comodule algebras
A
with a canonically
defined map
\chi:A\otimes_{B}A\to A\otimes H
which is required to be
invertible.

Definition 6.1
.

Let
H
be a Hopf algebra and let
A\in\mathcal{A}^{H}
be an
H
-comodule
algebra with coaction
\delta^{A}
.
Consider the subalgebra
B:=A^{coH}=\big\{b\in A\penalty\ |\penalty\ \delta^{A}(b)=b\otimes 1_{H}\big\}\subseteq A
of coinvariant elements
and let

A\otimes_{B}A

be the corresponding balanced tensor product with multiplication
m:A\otimes_{B}A\to A
induced from
m_{A}:A\otimes A\to A
.
The extension
B\subseteq A
is called an
H
-
Hopf–Galois extension
provided the
map

(6.1)

\displaystyle\chi:=(m\otimes\mathrm{id})\circ(\mathrm{id}\otimes_{B}\delta^{A}):A\otimes_{B}A\longrightarrow A\otimes H\penalty\ ,\quad a^{\prime}\otimes_{B}a\longmapsto a^{\prime}a_{\;(0)}\otimes a_{\;(1)},

the
canonical map
, is bijective.

Being
\chi
left
A
-linear, its inverse, when it exists, is determined by the translation map:

\tau={\chi^{-1}}{|_{{}_{1\otimes{H}}}}:\,1\otimes H\simeq H\to A\otimes_{B}A\,,\quad h\mapsto{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle{<1>}}\otimes{{h}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{<2>}}.

A Hopf–Galois extension is
cleft
if there is a convolution
invertible morphism of
H
-comodules
j:H\to A
(the
cleaving
map
), where
H
has coaction
\Delta
. This is equivalent to an isomorphism
A\simeq B\otimes H
of left

B
-modules and right
H
-comodules, where
B\otimes H
is a left

B
-module via multiplication on the left and a right H-comodule via
\mathrm{id}\otimes\Delta
.
When the cleaving map is an algebra map,
the algebra
A
is isomorphic to a smash product algebra
A\simeq B\sharp H
.

The extension
B=A^{coH}\subset A
is called

H
-
principal comodule algebra
if it is Hopf–Galois and

A
is
H
-equivariantly projective as a left
B
-module, i.e., there
exists a left
B
-module and right
H
-comodule morphism
s:A\to B\otimes A
that is a section of the (restricted) product
m:B\otimes A\to A
.
This is equivalent to requiring that the total space algebra
A
is
faithfully flat as a left
B
-module.
Cleft Hopf–Galois extensions are always principal comodule algebras.

6.2.
Gauge group

Let
{\rm{Hom}}_{\mathcal{M}^{H}}(A,A)
be the
\mathds{k}
-module of
H
-comodule maps from
A
to
A
and let
{\rm{Hom}}_{\mathcal{A}^{H}}(A,A)
be the subset of
algebra maps from
A
to
A
.
The following theorem improves previous results in
[
6
, Prop. 3.6]
(where
A
was quasi-commutative) and

[
23
, Prop. 3.3]
(where the faithfully flat
condition was used), see also
[
34
, Rem. 3.11]
.

Theorem 6.2
.

Let
B=A^{coH}\subseteq A
be an

H
-Hopf–Galois extension.
The set

(6.2)

\mathrm{Aut}_{B}(A):=\{\mathsf{F}\in{\rm{Hom}}_{\mathcal{A}^{H}}(A,A)\,|\,\,\mathsf{F}_{|_{B}}=\mathrm{id}\}

of right
H
-comodule algebra morphisms that restrict to the identity
on the subalgebra
B
is a group
with respect to the composition of maps

\mathsf{F}\cdot\mathsf{G}:=\mathsf{G}\circ\mathsf{F}

for all
\mathsf{F},\mathsf{G}\in\mathrm{Aut}_{B}(A)
. For
\mathsf{F}\in\mathrm{Aut}_{B}(A)
its
inverse
\mathsf{F}^{-1}\in\mathrm{Aut}_{B}(A)
is given by

(6.3)

\displaystyle\mathsf{F}^{-1}:=m\circ(\mathrm{id}\otimes m)\circ(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathsf{F}\otimes_{B}\mathrm{id})\circ(\mathrm{id}\otimes\tau)\circ\delta^{A}\>:A

\displaystyle\!\longrightarrow\!

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle a

\displaystyle\!\longmapsto\!

\displaystyle{a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(0)}}\mathsf{F}({{a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{<1>}}){{a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{<2>}}\penalty\ .\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\

Proof.

The map
\mathsf{F}^{-1}
in (
6.3
) is well-defined because
\mathsf{F}\otimes_{B}\mathrm{id}
is well-defined
due to the right
B
-linearity of
\mathsf{F}
.
The theorem is proven by showing that
\mathsf{F}^{-1}
is the inverse
of
\mathsf{F}
. We first prove that
\mathsf{F}^{-1}\circ\mathsf{F}=\mathrm{id}_{A}
.
Indeed, being
\mathsf{F}
an

H
-equivariant of algebra map,

(6.4)

\mathsf{F}^{-1}(\mathsf{F}(a))=\mathsf{F}({a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(0)}})\mathsf{F}({{a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{<1>}}){{a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{<2>}}=\mathsf{F}({a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(0)}}{{a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{<1>}}){{a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{<2>}}=\mathsf{F}(1)a=a\penalty\ .

Here in the last but one equality we used that
\chi
and its inverse are left

A
-linear
so that
{a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(0)}}\tau({a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}})={a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(0)}}\chi^{-1}(1\otimes{a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}})=\chi^{-1}({a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(0)}}\otimes{a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}})=\chi^{-1}(\chi(1\otimes{a}))=1\otimes a
.

To prove that
\mathsf{F}\circ\mathsf{F}^{-1}=\mathrm{id}_{A}
we notice that the balanced tensor
product map
\mathsf{F}\otimes_{B}\mathsf{F}:A\otimes_{B}A\to A\otimes_{B}A
is well defined because
\mathsf{F}
is
left and right
B
-linear. We show that

(\mathsf{F}\otimes_{B}\mathsf{F})\circ\tau=\tau\penalty\ .

This is equivalent to
\chi\circ(\mathsf{F}\otimes_{B}\mathsf{F})\circ\tau=1_{A}\otimes\mathrm{id}_{H}
; for all
h\in H
,

\begin{split}\chi\big(\mathsf{F}({h}^{\scriptscriptstyle{<1>}})\otimes_{B}\mathsf{F}({h}^{\scriptscriptstyle{<2>}})\big)&=\mathsf{F}({h}^{\scriptscriptstyle{<1>}})\,{\mathsf{F}({h}^{\scriptscriptstyle{<2>}})}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(0)}}\otimes{\mathsf{F}({h}^{\scriptscriptstyle{<2>}})}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\\[1.99997pt]
&=\mathsf{F}({h}^{\scriptscriptstyle{<1>}})\,\mathsf{F}({{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle{<2>}}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(0)}})\otimes{{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle{<2>}}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\\[1.99997pt]
&=\mathsf{F}({h}^{\scriptscriptstyle{<1>}}{{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle{<2>}}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(0)}})\otimes{{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle{<2>}}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\\[1.99997pt]
&=(\mathsf{F}\otimes\mathrm{id}_{H})(\chi({h}^{\scriptscriptstyle{<1>}}\otimes_{B}{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle{<2>}}))\\[1.99997pt]
&=1_{A}\otimes h\end{split}

where in the second equality we used
H
-equivariance of
\mathsf{F}
, in the
last that
\tau={\chi^{-1}}{|_{{}_{1\otimes{H}}}}
.

Using that
\mathsf{F}
is an algebra map and the above property it is
now easy to show that
\mathsf{F}\circ\mathsf{F}^{-1}=\mathsf{F}^{-1}\circ\mathsf{F}=\mathrm{id}_{A}
; for
all
a\in A
,

\begin{split}\mathsf{F}(\mathsf{F}^{-1}(a))&=\mathsf{F}\big({a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(0)}}\mathsf{F}({{a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{<1>}}){{a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{<2>}}\big)=\mathsf{F}({a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(0)}})\mathsf{F}(\mathsf{F}({{a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{<1>}}))\mathsf{F}({{a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{<2>}})\\[1.99997pt]
&=\mathsf{F}({a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(0)}})\mathsf{F}({{a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{<1>}}){{a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{<2>}}=\mathsf{F}^{-1}(\mathsf{F}(a))=a\penalty\ .\end{split}

where in the last but one equality we used equation (
6.4
).
∎

In general this notion of gauge group is too restrictive.
For instance, a Hopf algebra
H
coacting on itself with its coproduct, can be seen as the Hopf–Galois extension
of the ground field
\mathds{k}
.
In this case the gauge group
coincides with the group of
characters of
H
. Indeed for
\chi:H\to\mathds{k}
a
character, the map
\alpha_{\chi}:=(\chi\otimes\mathrm{id})\circ\Delta:H\to H
is
a right
H
-comodule algebra morphism. Vice versa, given a right

H
-comodule algebra morphism
\alpha
, the composition

\chi=\varepsilon\circ\alpha
is an algebra map. These two
constructions are one the inverse of the other.
This equivalence holds also for locally compact quantum groups
[
13
]
.
For example, the gauge group of the exention
\mathbb{C}\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{q}(SL(2))
,
q^{2}\not=1
, is
just the multiplicative group
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
.

More in general, for a faithfully flat Galois object
A
over
\mathds{k}
(a Hopf–Galois
extension
\mathds{k}=A^{coH}\subset A
)
we have
\mathrm{Aut}_{B}(A)={\rm{Char}}(L)
. This is the group of
characters of the Hopf algebra

L:=(A\otimes A)^{coH}
of coinvariant elements of the
H
-comodule algebra

A\otimes A
under the diagonal coaction
[
33
, §3.1]
,
[
23
, §6.1]
.

The gauge group
\mathrm{Aut}_{B}(A)
of an
H
-Hopf–Galois extension is in general
very small because, being a group, it is a classical object
associated to a noncommutative principal bundle.
Nonetheless in the relevant case of deformation quantization this notion is adeguate.
We show that for twist deformations of Hopf–Galois extensions, it
leads to a gauge group which is isomorphic to the
classical gauge group of the undeformed principal bundle.

Example 6.3
.

For simplicity we begin
with the Galois object

\mathds{k}\subseteq H
with
H
-coaction
given by the coproduct.
Let
H
be commutative with trivial cotriangular structure
R=\varepsilon\otimes\varepsilon
.
Let
\gamma:H\otimes H\to\mathds{k}
be a 2-cocycle on
H

with convolution inverse
\bar{\gamma}

(every twist on a Hopf algebra
dual to
H
gives a 2-cocycle on
H
via
\gamma(h\otimes h^{\prime})=\langle\mathsf{F}^{\alpha},h\rangle\,\langle\mathsf{F}_{\alpha},h^{\prime}\rangle
, see
e.g.
[
2
, App. A]
).
As in
[
6
, §4]

and dually to the theory in Section
4
, the Hopf
algebra
(H,R=\varepsilon\otimes\varepsilon)
is deformed to the
cotriangular Hopf algebra
(H_{\gamma},R_{\gamma}=\gamma_{21}*\bar{\gamma})
.
The total space algebra

(A=H,\cdot,\Delta)
is deformed as an
H
-comodule algebra to

(A_{\gamma}=H_{{\,\raisebox{1.5pt}{\scalebox{0.55}[0.55]{\mbox{$\bullet_{\mbox{$\gamma\,$}}$}}}}},{\,\raisebox{1.5pt}{\scalebox{0.55}[0.55]{\mbox{$\bullet_{\mbox{$\gamma\,$}}$}}}},\Delta)
, where

h{\,\raisebox{1.5pt}{\scalebox{0.55}[0.55]{\mbox{$\bullet_{\mbox{$\gamma\,$}}$}}}}h^{\prime}={h}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}{h^{\prime}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\bar{\gamma}({h}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\otimes{h^{\prime}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}})
for all
h,h^{\prime}\in H
.
We thus obtain the Hopf–Galois extension

\mathds{k}\subseteq H_{{\,\raisebox{1.5pt}{\scalebox{0.55}[0.55]{\mbox{$\bullet_{\mbox{$\gamma\,$}}$}}}}}
. This is a cleft
extension with cleaving map
j=\mathrm{id}_{H}:H_{\gamma}\to H_{{\,\raisebox{1.5pt}{\scalebox{0.55}[0.55]{\mbox{$\bullet_{\mbox{$\gamma\,$}}$}}}}}
, but
in general needs not be a trivial extension since
H_{\gamma}
and

H_{{\,\raisebox{1.5pt}{\scalebox{0.55}[0.55]{\mbox{$\bullet_{\mbox{$\gamma\,$}}$}}}}}
are in general not isomorphic as
H_{\gamma}
-comodule
algebras. The algebra
H_{{\,\raisebox{1.5pt}{\scalebox{0.55}[0.55]{\mbox{$\bullet_{\mbox{$\gamma\,$}}$}}}}}
is quasi-commutative:

h{{\,\raisebox{1.5pt}{\scalebox{0.55}[0.55]{\mbox{$\bullet_{\mbox{$\gamma\,$}}$}}}}}h^{\prime}={h^{\prime}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\!\!\>{\,\raisebox{1.5pt}{\scalebox{0.55}[0.55]{\mbox{$\bullet_{\mbox{$\gamma\,$}}$}}}}{h}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\penalty\ R_{\!}\left({{h}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}}\otimes{{h^{\prime}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}}\right)
for all
h,h^{\prime}\in H
.

The gauge group of this noncommutative cleft extension
\mathds{k}\subseteq H_{{\,\raisebox{1.5pt}{\scalebox{0.55}[0.55]{\mbox{$\bullet_{\mbox{$\gamma\,$}}$}}}}}
is
isomorphic to the gauge group of the trivial extension
\mathds{k}\subseteq H
. Indeed an algebra map
\mathsf{F}:H\to H
that is also a right

H
-comodule map satisfies
\Delta(\mathsf{F}(h))=\mathsf{F}({h}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}})\otimes{h}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}

and therefore

\displaystyle\mathsf{F}(h{\,\raisebox{1.5pt}{\scalebox{0.55}[0.55]{\mbox{$\bullet_{\mbox{$\gamma\,$}}$}}}}h^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{F}({h}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}{h^{\prime}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}})\bar{\gamma}({h}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\otimes{h^{\prime}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}})=\mathsf{F}({h}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}})\mathsf{F}({h^{\prime}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}})\bar{\gamma}({h}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\otimes{h^{\prime}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}})

(6.5)

\displaystyle={\mathsf{F}(h)}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}{\mathsf{F}(h^{\prime})}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\bar{\gamma}({\mathsf{F}(h)}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\otimes{\mathsf{F}(h^{\prime})}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}})=\mathsf{F}(h){\,\raisebox{1.5pt}{\scalebox{0.55}[0.55]{\mbox{$\bullet_{\mbox{$\gamma\,$}}$}}}}\mathsf{F}(h^{\prime})

for all
h,h^{\prime}\in H_{{\,\raisebox{1.5pt}{\scalebox{0.55}[0.55]{\mbox{$\bullet_{\mbox{$\gamma\,$}}$}}}}}
. This shows that
\mathsf{F}
is also an algebra map
\mathsf{F}:H_{{\,\raisebox{1.5pt}{\scalebox{0.55}[0.55]{\mbox{$\bullet_{\mbox{$\gamma\,$}}$}}}}}\to H_{{\,\raisebox{1.5pt}{\scalebox{0.55}[0.55]{\mbox{$\bullet_{\mbox{$\gamma\,$}}$}}}}}
and an
H_{\gamma}
-comodule algebra
map (since the coproduct of
H_{\gamma}
is the same as that of
H
).

For example, if
H=\mathcal{O}(SL(n))
,
the gauge group of the Hopf–Galois extension

\mathds{k}\subseteq\mathcal{O}(SL(n))_{{\,\raisebox{1.5pt}{\scalebox{0.55}[0.55]{\mbox{$\bullet_{\mbox{$\gamma\,$}}$}}}}}
is the classical gauge group
SL(n)
.

\blacksquare

This construction generalizes, thus extending the results of
[
6
, Prop. 4.12]
to the case where
A
is not necessarily quasi-commutative. A right
H
-comodule algebra
A
is deformed, via a 2-cocycle
\gamma
on
H
,
to the right

H_{\gamma}
-comodule algebra
A_{\gamma}
with product

(6.6)

a{\,\raisebox{1.5pt}{\scalebox{0.55}[0.55]{\mbox{$\bullet_{\mbox{$\gamma\,$}}$}}}}a^{\prime}={a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}{a^{\prime}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\bar{\gamma}({a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\otimes{a^{\prime}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}})

for all
a,a^{\prime}\in A
. The subalgebra
B\subseteq A
of
H
-coinvariant
elements is also the subalgebra of
A_{\gamma}
of

H_{\gamma}
-coinvariant elements. Then
B\subseteq A_{\gamma}
is an

H_{\gamma}
-Hopf–Galois extension if and only if
B\subseteq A
is an

H
-Hopf–Galois extension (
[
2
, Corol. 3.7]
).

Proposition 6.4
.

Let
B=A^{coH}\subseteq A
be a Hopf–Galois extension and
\gamma

be a 2-cocycle on
H
. The gauge group
\mathrm{Aut}_{B}(A_{\gamma})
of the twisted
Hopf–Galois extension
B=A_{\gamma}^{coH_{\gamma}}\subseteq A_{\gamma}
is
isomorphic to the gauge group
\mathrm{Aut}_{B}(A)
of the original Hopf–Galois extension.

Proof.

Let
\mathsf{F}:A\to A
be a unital algebra map, then the same
\mathds{k}
-linear
map
\mathsf{F}
thought as a map
\mathsf{F}:A_{\gamma}\to A_{\gamma}
is a unital algebra
map. Indeed the unit of
A_{\gamma}
and of
A
are the same. The
algebra property

\mathsf{F}(a{\,\raisebox{1.5pt}{\scalebox{0.55}[0.55]{\mbox{$\bullet_{\mbox{$\gamma\,$}}$}}}}a^{\prime})=\mathsf{F}(a){\,\raisebox{1.5pt}{\scalebox{0.55}[0.55]{\mbox{$\bullet_{\mbox{$\gamma\,$}}$}}}}\mathsf{F}(a^{\prime})
for
a,a^{\prime}\in A_{\gamma}

is shown as in (
6.5
).
∎

In particular we have the following example (cf.
[
6
, Ex. 4.19]
).

Example 6.5
.

Consider the Hopf–Galois extension
B=A^{coH}\subseteq A
, with
A=\mathcal{O}(P)
,
B=P/G
,
H=\mathcal{O}(G)
, of
a principal
G
-bundle
P\to P/G

of affine varieties. Its twisted deformation

B=A_{\gamma}^{coH_{\gamma}}\subseteq A_{\gamma}

has gauge group isomorphic to the one of
P\to P/G
.

\blacksquare

7.
Braided Hopf algebra of gauge transformations

We study the gauge symmetry
of a
K
-equivariant Hopf–Galois extension,
for
K
a triangular Hopf algebra.
This is an
H
-Hopf Galois extension
B=A^{coH}\subseteq A
with

A
a
K
-equivariant
H
-comodule algebra, as defined in Section

2.2
, and
B
a
K
-submodule (this holds for example
when
K
is flat as

\mathds{k}
-module, cf.
[
2
, Prop. 3.12]
).
In this context there is a
K
-braided Hopf algebra of (infinitesimal) gauge transformations that
generalizes the notion of gauge group
of the previous section.

According to the definition of gauge group
\mathrm{Aut}_{B}(A)
in (
6.2
), infinitesimal gauge
transformations of a Hopf–Galois extension
B=A^{coH}\subseteq A

are
H
-comodule maps that are vertical derivations

\displaystyle\mathrm{aut}_{B}(A):=\{u\in{\rm{Hom}}(A,A)\;|\;

\displaystyle\delta(u(a))=u({a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(0)}})\otimes{a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}},

(7.1)

\displaystyle u(aa^{\prime})=u(a)a^{\prime}+au(a^{\prime})\,,

\displaystyle u(b)=0,\mbox{ for all }a,a^{\prime}\in A,b\in B\}\penalty\ .

They form a Lie algebra with Lie bracket given by the commutator.

In the
K
-equivariant case
we can require a braided derivation property.

Definition 7.1
.

Let
(K,\mathsf{R})
be a triangular Hopf algebra. Infinitesimal gauge
transformations of a
K
-equivariant Hopf–Galois extension
B=A^{coH}\subseteq A

are
H
-comodule maps that are braided vertical derivations

\displaystyle\mathrm{aut}^{\mathsf{R}}_{B}(A):=\{u\in{\rm{Hom}}(A,A)\;|\;

\displaystyle\delta(u(a))=u({a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(0)}})\otimes{a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}},

(7.2)

\displaystyle u(aa^{\prime})=u(a)a^{\prime}+(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright a)(\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright u)(a^{\prime})\,,

\displaystyle u(b)=0,\mbox{ for all }a,a^{\prime}\in A,b\in B\}\penalty\ .

In this context infinitesimal
gauge transformations form a
Lie algebra object in the category of
K
-modules.
For a
(K,\mathsf{R})
-module algebra
A
,
recall that

{\rm{Der}}^{\mathsf{R}}(A)
as in (
5.3
) denotes the braided Lie algebra of braided derivations of

A
.

Proposition 7.2
.

The linear space
\mathrm{aut}^{\mathsf{R}}_{B}(A)
with bracket

\displaystyle[\penalty\ ,\penalty\ ]_{\mathsf{R}}:\mathrm{aut}^{\mathsf{R}}_{B}(A)\otimes\mathrm{aut}^{\mathsf{R}}_{B}(A)

\displaystyle\to\mathrm{aut}^{\mathsf{R}}_{B}(A)

(7.3)

\displaystyle u\otimes u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\mapsto[u,u^{\prime}]_{\mathsf{R}}:=u\circ u^{\prime}-\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright u^{\prime}\circ\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright u\penalty\ ,\penalty\ \penalty\

for all
u,u^{\prime}\in\mathrm{aut}^{\mathsf{R}}_{B}(A)
, is a
K
-braided Lie subalgebra of

{\rm{Der}}^{\mathsf{R}}(A)
.

Proof.

We show that
\mathrm{aut}^{\mathsf{R}}_{B}(A)
is the intersection of three
K
-modules.
Firstly,
the
\mathds{k}
-submodule
{\rm{Hom}}_{\mathcal{M}^{H}}(A,A)\subseteq{\rm{Hom}}(A,A)
of

H
-equivariant
\mathds{k}
-linear maps from
A
to
A
is a

K
-submodule of
{\rm{Hom}}(A,A)
. Indeed commutativity of
the
K
-action with the
H
-coaction on
A
is equivalent to
H
-equivariance,

\delta({\mathcal{L}}_{k}a)=({\mathcal{L}}_{k}\otimes\,\mathrm{id}_{A})\delta(a)
,
of the map
{\mathcal{L}}_{k}:A\to A
,
{\mathcal{L}}_{k}(a)=k\vartriangleright a

for all
k\in K
and
a\in A
.
Then
for
u\in{\rm{Hom}}_{\mathcal{M}^{H}}(A,A)
and from
(k\vartriangleright_{{\rm{Hom}}(A,A)}u)(a)={k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\vartriangleright(u(S({k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}})\vartriangleright a))
for all
a\in A
(cf. (
2.1
)), we
have

k\vartriangleright_{{\rm{Hom}}(A,A)}u={\mathcal{L}}_{{k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}}\circ\,u\circ{\mathcal{L}}_{S({k}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}})}\in{\rm{Hom}}_{\mathcal{M}^{H}}(A,A)
since composition of
H
-equivariant
maps.

Since
B
is a
K
-module, the

\mathds{k}
-submodule
{\rm{Hom}}_{B}(A,A)\subseteq{\rm{Hom}}(A,A)
of linear maps
that annihilate
B
(that is of vertical maps) is a

K
-submodule of
{\rm{Hom}}(A,A)
. Hence

\mathrm{aut}^{\mathsf{R}}_{B}(A)={\rm{Hom}}_{\mathcal{M}^{H}}(A,A)\cap{\rm{Der}}^{\mathsf{R}}(A)\cap{\rm{Hom}}_{B}(A,A)
is a
K
-submodule
since intersection of
K
-submodules.

The bracket in (
7.2
) is just the restriction to
\mathrm{aut}^{\mathsf{R}}_{B}(A)
of the one of
{\rm{Der}}^{\mathsf{R}}(A)
defined in (
5.7
).
It closes in
\mathrm{aut}^{\mathsf{R}}_{B}(A)
: being the braided
Lie bracket of
{\rm{Der}}^{\mathsf{R}}(A)
a braided commutator,
H
-equivariance and verticality follow.
∎

In this braided context infinitesimal gauge transformations of a
K
-equivariant Hopf–Galois extension
B=A^{coH}\subseteq A
are thus encoded in the braided Lie algebra
\mathrm{aut}^{\mathsf{R}}_{B}(A)
, or
equivalently, in the braided Hopf algebra

\mathcal{U}(\mathrm{aut}^{\mathsf{R}}_{B}(A))
associated with
\mathrm{aut}^{\mathsf{R}}_{B}(A)

( cf. Corollary
5.12
).
We recover the Lie algebra (
7.1
)
of (usual) vertical derivations when
K=\mathds{k}
with
\mathsf{R}=1\otimes 1
.

7.1.
Quantum principal bundle over quantum homogeneous
space

A quantum subgroup of a Hopf algebra
A
is a Hopf algebra
H
together with
a surjective bialgebra (and thus Hopf algebra) homomorphism
\pi:A\to H
. Then

A
is a right
H
-comodule algebra via the projection of the coproduct

(7.4)

\delta:=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\pi)\circ\Delta:A\longrightarrow A\otimes H\penalty\ .

When

B=A^{coH}\subseteq A
is a Hopf–Galois extension, we call

A
a
quantum principal bundle
over the quantum homogeneous space

B
(see e.g.
[
12
, §5.1]
).

As in Example
5.10
, let
A
be cotriangular with dual
Hopf algebra
U
that is triangular with
\mathscr{R}\in U\otimes U

(here a topological tensor product is understood if
U
is not finite dimensional over
\mathds{k}
).
From the
U^{op}\otimes U
-action

\vartriangleright:U^{op}\otimes U\otimes A\to A
,
(\zeta\otimes\xi)\vartriangleright a=\langle\zeta,{a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}\rangle{a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\langle\xi,{a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(3)}}\rangle
the right coaction of
A
on itself
\Delta:A\to A\otimes A
and the

U^{op}
-action commute, hence so do the right
H
-coaction
\delta:A\to A\otimes H
and the
U^{op}
-action (this is in general not the case for the
U
-action).
It follows that the
quantum principal bundle
A

over the quantum homogeneous space
B
is a

K
-equivariant Hopf–Galois extension
B=A^{coH}\subseteq A
with
(K,\mathsf{R})=(U^{op},\overline{\mathscr{R}})
.
The associated
K
-braided Lie algebra of infinitesimal gauge
transformations is
\mathrm{aut}^{\mathsf{R}}_{B}(A)\subseteq\rm{Der}^{\mathsf{R}}{(A)}
.

We study the relation between

\mathrm{aut}^{\mathsf{R}}_{B}(A)
and the braided Lie algebra
of braided derivations

{\rm{Der}}^{\mathfrak{R}\!\!\>}(A)=A\otimes{\rm{Der}}^{\mathfrak{R}\!\!\>}(A){}_{\rm{inv}}
of the cotriangular Hopf algebra

A
in Example
5.10
.
Let
(H,R_{H})
be cotriangular and
the cotriangular structure on
A
be given by that on
H
, that is,

R(a\otimes a^{\prime})=R_{H}(\pi(a)\otimes\pi(a^{\prime}))
for all
a,a^{\prime}\in A
.
In terms of the triangular structure
(U_{H},\mathscr{R}_{H})
dual to

(H,R_{H})
we have
U_{H}\subset U
and
\mathscr{R}_{H}=\mathscr{R}\in U_{H}\otimes U_{H}\subset U\otimes U
.
This implies
\mathsf{R}_{H}:=\overline{\mathscr{R}}_{H}=\overline{\mathscr{R}}

so that

(7.5)

{\rm{Der}}^{\mathsf{R}_{H}\!\!\>}(A)={\rm{Der}}^{\mathsf{R}}(A)

as linear spaces of braided derivations; the first is a
K_{H}=U_{H}^{op}
-braided
Lie algebra, the second is a
K=U^{op}
-braided Lie algebra.
Similarly,
\mathfrak{R}_{H}=\mathfrak{R}
, so that

{\rm{Der}}^{\mathfrak{R}_{H}\!\!\>}(A)={\rm{Der}}^{\mathfrak{R}\!\!\>}(A)

as linear spaces; the first is a
U_{H}^{op}\otimes U_{H}
-braided
Lie algebra, the second is a
U^{op}\otimes U
-braided one.
The linear subspace of

H
-equivariant derivations of
A
,

{\rm{Der}}^{\mathfrak{R}_{H}\!\!\>}_{{\!\mathcal{M}}^{H}\>\!\!}(A)=\{u\in{\rm{Der}}^{\mathfrak{R}_{H}\!\!\>}(A)\,|\,\delta(u(a))=u({a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(0)}})\otimes{a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}},{\mbox{ for all }}a\in A\}

is a
U_{H}^{op}\otimes U_{H}
-braided Lie subalgebra of

{\rm{Der}}^{\mathfrak{R}_{H}\!\!\>}(A)
. Indeed
the action of
U_{H}
is trivial
while the action of
U_{H}^{op}
closes in

{\rm{Der}}^{\mathfrak{R}\!\!\>}_{{\!\mathcal{M}}^{H}\>\!\!}(A)
, with
proofs similar to those for

{\rm{Der}}^{\mathfrak{R}\!\!\>}(A){}_{\rm{inv}}
in Example
5.10
.

Theorem 7.3
.

Let
(A,R)
be a cotriangular Hopf algebra with dual triangular Hopf
algebra
(U,\mathscr{R})
. Let

B=A^{coH}\subseteq A
be
a quantum principal bundle
over the quantum homogeneous space
B
,
with Hopf algebra projection
\pi:A\to H
.
Assume
(H,R_{H})
is cotriangular with

R=R_{H}\circ(\pi\otimes\pi
).

Then the braided gauge transformations
\mathrm{aut}^{\mathsf{R}}_{B}(A)
are the
vertical braided vector fields in

B\otimes{\rm{Der}}^{\mathfrak{R}\!\!\>}(A){}_{\rm{inv}}
,
where

{\rm{Der}}^{\mathfrak{R}\!\!\>}(A){}_{\rm{inv}}
are the
right-invariant vector fields defining the
bicovariant differential calculus on
(A,R)
.
This linear space isomorphism is a
U^{op}_{H}
-braided Lie algebra
isomorphism, where
U_{H}\subset U
is the triangular Hopf algebra dual to
H
.

Proof.

Recall from Example
5.10
that
{\rm{Der}}^{\mathfrak{R}\!\!\>}(A)=A\otimes{\rm{Der}}^{\mathfrak{R}\!\!\>}(A){}_{\rm{inv}}
.
Here
{\rm{Der}}^{\mathfrak{R}\!\!\>}(A){}_{\rm{inv}}
are the right-invariant vector fields for the

A
-coaction (and thus for the
H
-coaction); they are
H
-equivariant.
Then,

{\rm{Der}}^{\mathfrak{R}\!\!\>}_{{\!\mathcal{M}}^{H}\>\!\!}(A)=B\otimes{\rm{Der}}^{\mathfrak{R}\!\!\>}(A){}_{\rm{inv}}\penalty\ .

Recall the linear space equalities

{\rm{Der}}^{\mathfrak{R}\!\!\>}(A)={\rm{Der}}^{\mathfrak{R}_{H}\!\!\>}(A)

and the analogous one in (
7.5
).
From the proof of Proposition
7.2
,

{\rm{Der}}^{\mathsf{R}\!\!\>}_{{\!\mathcal{M}}^{H}\>\!\!}(A)={\rm{Der}}^{\mathsf{R}}(A)\cap{\rm{Hom}}_{\mathcal{M}^{H}}(A,A)

is a
U^{op}
-braided Lie algebra and thus
can be seen as the
U_{H}^{op}
-braided Lie algebra

{\rm{Der}}^{\mathsf{R}_{H}\!\!\>}_{{\!\mathcal{M}}^{H}\>\!\!}(A)={\rm{Der}}^{\mathsf{R}_{H}}(A)\cap{\rm{Hom}}_{\mathcal{M}^{H}}(A,A)
.
The equality of
U_{H}^{op}
-braided Lie algebras

{\rm{Der}}^{\mathsf{R}_{H}\!\!\>}_{{\!\mathcal{M}}^{H}\>\!\!}(A)={\rm{Der}}^{\mathfrak{R}_{H}\!\!\>}_{{\!\mathcal{M}}^{H}\>\!\!}(A)

is immediate since the
U_{H}
-action on

{\rm{Der}}^{\mathfrak{R}_{H}\!\!\>}_{{\!\mathcal{M}}^{H}\>\!\!}(A)
is
trivial so that the braiding
{\mathfrak{R}_{H}\!\!\>}
acts
as
\mathsf{R}_{H}
. We thus have the
U^{op}_{H}
-braided Lie algebra isomorphism

{\rm{Der}}^{\mathsf{R}_{H}\!\!\>}_{{\!\mathcal{M}}^{H}\>\!\!}(A)=B\otimes{\rm{Der}}^{\mathfrak{R}\!\!\>}(A){}_{\rm{inv}}\penalty\ .

The theorem is proven restricting this isomorphism to vertical vector fields.
∎

Given a cotriangular Hopf algebra
A
the Galois object

\mathds{k}=A^{coA}\subseteq A
is a quantum principal
bundle over the quantum homogeneous space
B=\mathds{k}
.
Here
(A,R)=(H,R_{H})
so that,

Corollary 7.4
.

Let
(A,R)
be a cotriangular Hopf algebra with dual triangular Hopf
algebra
(U,\mathscr{R})
.
The
U^{op}
-braided Lie algebra of infinitesimal gauge trasformations
{\rm{aut}}^{\mathsf{R}}_{\mathds{k}}(A)
of the Galois object

\mathds{k}=A^{coA}\subseteq A
is
isomorphic to the
U^{op}
-braided Lie algebra
{\rm{Der}}^{\mathfrak{R}\!\!\>}(A){}_{\rm{inv}}
of right-invariant vector fields which
define the bicovariant differential calculus on
(A,R)
.

This theorem and the corollary show that
Definition
7.1
of braided infinitesimal
gauge transformations
\mathrm{aut}^{\mathsf{R}}_{B}(A)
captures a much richer structure
– related to the bicovariant differential calculus -- than
that of the Lie algebra
{\rm{aut}}_{B}(A)
in (
7.1
).

Example 7.5
.

Cotriangular quantum group
\mathcal{O}(U_{q}(2))

(the multiparametric quantum group
\mathcal{O}(U_{q,r}(2))
with
r=1
, see
e.g.
[
32
]
).
We recall that
\mathcal{O}(U_{q}(2))
is generated by the entries of the matrix

T_{\!}=\!\!\>(t_{ij})\!:=

\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix},

and by the inverse
D^{-1}
of the quantum determinant
D=ad-q^{-1}bc
. They satisfy the commutation relations

\displaystyle ab=q^{-1}ba\,\,;\quad ac=qca\,\,;\quad bd=qdb\,\,;\quad cd=q^{-1}dc\,\,;\quad bc=q^{2}cb\,\,;\quad ad=da\,

\displaystyle a{D}^{-1}={D}^{-1}a\;;\quad b{D}^{-1}=q^{-2}{D}^{-1}b\;;\quad c{D}^{-1}={q}^{2}{D}^{-1}c\;;\quad d{D}^{-1}={D}^{-1}d\,.\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\

The costructures are

\Delta(t_{ij})=\sum_{k=1,2}t_{ik}{\otimes}t_{kj}
,
\Delta(D^{-1})=D^{-1}\otimes D^{-1}
, and

\varepsilon(t_{ij})=\delta_{ij}
,
\varepsilon(D^{-1})=1
,
while the antipode is

S(T)={D}^{-1}{\mbox{\tiny{$\begin{pmatrix}d&-q^{-1}b\\
-qc&a\end{pmatrix}$}}}\,,\penalty\ S(D^{-1})=D\,.

The
*
-structure defining the real form
\mathcal{O}(U_{q}(2))
requires the
deformation parameter to be a phase;
the
*
-structure is then given
by

{\mbox{\tiny{$\begin{pmatrix}a^{*}&b^{*}\\
{}c^{*}&d^{*}\end{pmatrix}$}}}={D}^{-1}{\mbox{\tiny{$\begin{pmatrix}d&-qc\\
-q^{-1}b&a\end{pmatrix}$}}}\,,\penalty\ (D^{-1})^{*}=D
.

From these relations it is easy to see that

{\rm{Aut}}_{\mathds{k}}(\mathcal{O}(U_{q}(2)))={\rm{Char}}(\mathcal{O}(U_{q}(2)))=U(1)\times U(1)

with abelian gauge Lie algebra

{\rm{aut}}_{\mathds{k}}{{\mathcal{O}(U_{q}(2))}}=\mathbb{R}^{2}
. This is half the vector space dimension of the
commutative case (
q=1
). Definition
7.1

gives on the other hand a braided gauge Lie algebra

{\rm{aut}}_{\mathds{k}}^{\mathsf{R}}(\mathcal{O}(U_{q}(2)))
that is non-abelian and 4-dimensional since
the bicovariant differential calculus on
\mathcal{O}(U_{q}(2))
is
4-dimensional. Indeed, since
\mathcal{O}(U_{q}(2))=\mathcal{O}(U(2))_{\mathsf{F}}
is a twist deformation of the coordinate ring on

U(2)

[
32
]
, the calculus can be obtained as twist deformation of the calculus on
U(2)
,
hence from the linear space equalities

\mathrm{Der}{(\mathcal{O}(U(2)))}=\mathrm{Der}{(\mathcal{O}(U(2)))}_{\mathsf{F}}=\mathrm{Der}{(\mathcal{O}(U(2))_{\mathsf{F}})}
, cf. Theorem

5.19
,
the linear space of vector fields has classical dimension.
The four dimensional quantum Lie algebra is explicitly
given in
[
5
]
, Table 1 with conjugation
iii) in (4.69) there. Notice that this is not a twist deformation of
a principal bundle as in Example (
6.3
), rather
\mathcal{O}(U_{q}(2))
it is a deformation of
\mathcal{O}(U(2))

as a Hopf algebra.

\blacksquare

Remark 7.6
.

Braided derivations for a quantum principal bundle
A
over a quantum
homogoneous space
B=A^{coH}
can be more intrinsically defined in
terms of the cotriangular Hopf algebra
(A,R)
, without referring to a
dual triangular Hopf algebra
(U,\mathscr{R})
giving
(K,\mathsf{R})=(U^{op},\overline{\mathscr{R}})
. Indeed, from Definition
5.3
of braided
derivation, we have,

(\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}\vartriangleright a)\!\;(\mathsf{R}^{\alpha}\vartriangleright\psi)(a^{\prime})=\bar{R}_{\alpha}({a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}){R}_{\beta}({a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}){a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(3)}}\!\>R^{\beta}({a^{\prime}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}})\bar{R}^{\alpha}({\psi({a^{\prime}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}})}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}})\,{\psi({a^{\prime}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}})}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}

for all
a,a^{\prime}\in A
,

\psi\in{\rm{Hom}}(A,A)
. Here
we used the adjoint
U^{op}
-action on
{\rm{Hom}}(A,A)
, the properties of the universal matrix
\mathsf{R}

and that
\bar{R}(a\otimes a^{\prime})=\langle\overline{\mathscr{R}},a\otimes a^{\prime}\rangle=\langle\mathsf{R},a\otimes a^{\prime}\rangle
, for all
a,a^{\prime}\in A
.
Therefore, for
(A,R)
cotriangular we have the
R
-braided derivations

\displaystyle{\rm{Der}}^{R}(A)=\{\psi:A\to A\,|\;\psi

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\mbox{is $\mathds{k}$-linear},\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \;\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\

\displaystyle\psi(aa^{\prime})=

\displaystyle\!\!\!\psi(a)a^{\prime}+\bar{R}_{\alpha}({a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}}){R}_{\beta}({a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}){a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(3)}}R^{\beta}({a^{\prime}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}})\bar{R}^{\alpha}({\psi({a^{\prime}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}})}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}})\,{\psi({a^{\prime}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}})}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(2)}}\}

with infinitesimal gauge transformations

{\rm{aut}}_{B}^{R\!\!\>}(A)=\{u\in{\rm{Der}}^{R}(A)\,|\,\delta(u(a))=u({a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(0)}})\otimes{a}_{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}},u(b)=0,\mbox{ for all }a\in A,b\in B\}.

These are expressed only in terms of
the cotriangular Hopf algebra
(A,R)
and the Hopf–Galois extension
B:=A^{coH}\subseteq A
with coaction given in (
7.4
).

When
A
is finite dimensional its cotriangularity is equivalent
to the triangularity of its dual Hopf algebra
U
so that
{\rm{Der}}^{R}(A)={\rm{Der}}^{\mathsf{R}}(A)
and

{\rm{aut}}_{B}^{R\!\!\>}(A)=\mathrm{aut}^{\mathsf{R}}_{B}(A)
as defined in Definition
7.1

with
(K,\mathsf{R})=(U^{op},{\overline{\mathscr{R}}})
.

8.
Braided Hopf algebra gauge symmetry from twist deformation

Given a twist
\mathsf{F}
of the Hopf algebra
K
, the
K
-equivariant
H
-comodule algebra

A
is twisted to the
K_{\mathsf{F}}
-equivariant
H
-comodule algebra
A_{\mathsf{F}}

with multiplication defined in (
4
). The
K
-submodule
algebra
B=A^{coH}
of
A
is twisted to the
K_{\mathsf{F}}
-submodule
algebra
B_{\mathsf{F}}=A_{\mathsf{F}}^{coH}
. Furthermore
B=A^{coH}\subseteq A
is a
Hopf–Galois extension (principal comodule algebra) if and only if
B_{\mathsf{F}}=A_{\mathsf{F}}^{coH}\subseteq A_{\mathsf{F}}
is a Hopf–Galois extension (principal comodule algebra), see

[
2
, Corol. 3.16 (Corol. 3.19)]
.

We study
the twist deformation of infinitesimal gauge transformations when
K

has triangular structure
\mathsf{R}
using the results in §
5.4
.
In this case
K_{\mathsf{F}}
has triangular structure
\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}
and the
K
-braided Lie algebra
(\mathrm{aut}^{\mathsf{R}}_{B}(A),[\penalty\ ,\penalty\ ])

is twisted to the

K_{\mathsf{F}}
-braided Lie algebra
(\mathrm{aut}^{\mathsf{R}}_{B}(A)_{\mathsf{F}},[\penalty\ ,\penalty\ ]_{\mathsf{F}})
with bracket

[\penalty\ ,\penalty\ ]_{\mathsf{F}}=[\penalty\ ,\penalty\ ]
as in Proposition
5.14
. This is a braided Lie subalgebra of

(\rm{Der}^{\mathsf{R}}(A)_{\mathsf{F}},[\penalty\ ,\penalty\ ]_{\mathsf{F}})
. We can also consider the
K_{\mathsf{F}}
-braided Lie
algebra
(\mathrm{aut}^{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{B_{\mathsf{F}}}(A_{\mathsf{F}}),[\penalty\ ,\penalty\ ]_{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}})
:

Proposition 8.1
.

The
(K_{\mathsf{F}},\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}})
-braided Lie algebras
(\mathrm{aut}^{\mathsf{R}}_{B}(A)_{\mathsf{F}},[\penalty\ ,\penalty\ ]_{\mathsf{F}})
and

(\mathrm{aut}^{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{B_{\mathsf{F}}}(A_{\mathsf{F}}),

[\penalty\ ,\penalty\ ]_{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}})
are isomorphic via the map

\mathcal{D}:\mathrm{aut}^{\mathsf{R}}_{B}(A)_{\mathsf{F}}{\,\longrightarrow\,}\mathrm{aut}^{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{B_{\mathsf{F}}}(A_{\mathsf{F}}),

the restriction of the isomorphism
\mathcal{D}:(\rm{Der}^{\mathsf{R}}{(A)}_{\mathsf{F}},[\penalty\ ,\penalty\ ]_{\mathsf{F}}){\,\to\,}(\rm{Der}^{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}{(A_{\mathsf{F}})},[\penalty\ ,\penalty\ ]_{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}})
of braided
Lie algebras, in Theorem
5.19
, to
\mathrm{aut}^{\mathsf{R}}_{B}(A)_{\mathsf{F}}\subseteq\rm{Der}^{\mathsf{R}}(A)_{\mathsf{F}}
.

Proof.

Since
B_{\mathsf{F}}
is a
K_{\mathsf{F}}
-module (and a
K
-module since
the
K_{\mathsf{F}}
and
K
algebra structures coincide), the isomorphism
\mathcal{D}
maps vertical derivations into vertical
derivations:
\mathcal{D}(u)(b)=0
for any
u\in\mathrm{aut}^{\mathsf{R}}_{B}(A)_{\mathsf{F}}
and
b\in B_{\mathsf{F}}
. Equivariance of
D(u)
under the

H
-coaction is straighforward from
H
-equivariance of
u
and the
compatibility of the
K
-action (or the
K_{\mathsf{F}}
-action) with the

H
-coaction (cf. (
2.11
)). Verticality and
H
-equivariance
of
\mathcal{D}^{-1}(u_{\mathsf{F}})
for any
u_{\mathsf{F}}\in\mathrm{aut}^{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{B_{\mathsf{F}}}(A_{\mathsf{F}})
are similarly proven.
As a consequence
\mathcal{D}:\mathrm{aut}^{\mathsf{R}}_{B}(A)_{\mathsf{F}}{\,\to\,}\mathrm{aut}^{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{B_{\mathsf{F}}}(A_{\mathsf{F}})\subseteq\rm{Der}^{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}{(A_{\mathsf{F}})}
is
injective and surjective.
∎

We can also consider
the
(K,\mathsf{R})
-braided Hopf algebra

\mathcal{U}(\mathrm{aut}^{\mathsf{R}}_{B}(A))
. Under a twist
\mathsf{F}
of
K
we have the
(K_{\mathsf{F}},\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}})
-braided Hopf algebra

\mathcal{U}(\mathrm{aut}^{\mathsf{R}}_{B}(A))_{\mathsf{F}}
:

Corollary 8.2
.

The twist
\mathcal{U}(\mathrm{aut}^{\mathsf{R}}_{B}(A))_{\mathsf{F}}
of the
(K,\mathsf{R})
-braided Hopf algebra
\mathcal{U}(\mathrm{aut}^{\mathsf{R}}_{B}(A))
of the Hopf–Galois extension
B=A^{coH}\subseteq A
is isomorphic to the
(K_{\mathsf{F}},\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}})
-braided Hopf algebra

\mathcal{U}(\mathrm{aut}^{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{B_{\mathsf{F}}}(A_{\mathsf{F}}))
of the Hopf–Galois extension

B_{\mathsf{F}}=A_{\mathsf{F}}^{coH}\subseteq A_{\mathsf{F}}
.

Proof.

From Proposition
5.18
the braided Hopf algebra
\mathcal{U}(\mathrm{aut}^{\mathsf{R}}_{B}(A))_{\mathsf{F}}
is
isomorphic to
\mathcal{U}(\mathrm{aut}^{\mathsf{R}}_{B}(A)_{\mathsf{F}})
. As a corollary of Proposition

8.1
this latter is isomorphic to the
(K_{\mathsf{F}},\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}})
-braided Hopf
algebra
\mathcal{U}(\mathrm{aut}^{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{B_{\mathsf{F}}}(A_{\mathsf{F}}))
.
∎

8.1.
Gauge transformations of twisted principal bundles

Braided Lie algebra and Hopf algebra gauge symmetries
arise in particular by considering twist deformations of
commutative principal bundles of affine varieties.
In this case
K=\mathcal{U}({\rm{Lie}}(L))
is a cocommutative Hopf algebra and
H=\mathcal{O}(G)
a commutative
Hopf algebra with
L
and
G
affine algebraic groups and
\mathcal{O}(G)
the
coordinate ring of
G
. The
L
-equivariant principal
bundle of affine varieties
P\to M=P/G
gives the
(K,\mathsf{R}=1\otimes 1)
-equivariant Hopf–Galois extension
B=A^{coH}\subseteq A
, where
A=\mathcal{O}(P)
,
B=\mathcal{O}(M)
are the coordinate rings of

P
and
M
. The Lie algebra
\mathrm{aut}_{B}(A)
and the Hopf algebra

\mathcal{U}(\mathrm{aut}_{B}(A))
of infinitesimal gauge transformations are
braided Lie and Hopf algebras with trivial braiding
\mathsf{R}=1\otimes 1
. Under a twist
\mathsf{F}
of
K

we obtain the
(K_{\mathsf{F}},\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}=\mathsf{F}_{21}\mathsf{F}^{-1})
-braided Lie and Hopf algebras

\mathrm{aut}^{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{B_{\mathsf{F}}}(A_{\mathsf{F}})
and
\mathcal{U}(\mathrm{aut}^{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{B_{\mathsf{F}}}(A_{\mathsf{F}}))
of the Hopf–Galois extension

B_{\mathsf{F}}=A_{\mathsf{F}}^{coH}\subseteq A_{\mathsf{F}}
.

Due to the isomorphisms

\mathrm{aut}^{\mathsf{R}}_{B}(A)_{\mathsf{F}}\simeq\mathrm{aut}^{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{B_{\mathsf{F}}}(A_{\mathsf{F}})
and
\mathcal{U}(\mathrm{aut}^{\mathsf{R}}_{B}(A))_{\mathsf{F}}\simeq\mathcal{U}(\mathrm{aut}^{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{F}}}_{B_{\mathsf{F}}}(A_{\mathsf{F}}))

of
Proposition
8.1
and Corollary
8.2

these braided Lie and Hopf algebras coincide as linear spaces
with the initial Lie and Hopf algebras
\mathrm{aut}_{B}(A)
and

\mathcal{U}(\mathrm{aut}_{B}(A))
.
This has to be compared with the (unbraided) gauge Lie
and Hopf algebras

\mathrm{aut}_{B}(A_{\mathsf{F}})
and
\mathcal{U}(\mathrm{aut}_{B}(A_{\mathsf{F}}))
as defined in (
7.1
) which
are usually much smaller as vector spaces (cf. Example

7.5
).

In the following we illustrate this constructions with a few explicit examples considering
twists arising from actions of tori,
L=\mathbb{T}^{2}
(abelian twists),
and from actions of
L=\mathbb{R}_{>0}\ltimes\mathbb{R}
,
the affine group
ax+b
of the real line
(Jordanian twists).

Let us start with a
\mathbb{T}^{2}
-equivariant principal bundle
P\to M=P/G

as before. The universal enveloping algebra
K
of

\mathbb{T}^{2}
is
generated by two commuting elements
H_{1},H_{2}
. Due to the torus
action, these act as derivations on
A
and on
B
. The twist

(8.1)

\mathsf{F}=e^{{\pi}i\theta(H_{1}\otimes H_{2}-H_{2}\otimes H_{1})}

of
K
, with
\theta\in\mathbb{R}
